# CHAT.md — the chat, kept in the repository

> "Chat is refusing to rewind and even providing any handoff thats why i wish there is something
> like merge, so that you continue automatic" ... "you must have that chat.md to understand all
> my flow" — the owner, 11 November 2026

A chat can end with no warning. The one that ran this repository from 4 August to 11 November
2026 grew past its size limit: it could not be rewound and it could not write a handoff. So the
handoff is no longer something a chat writes at the end. **It is this file, and it is kept true
all the time** — every change updates it in the same commit, and `test/chat.test.mjs` fails when
it falls behind the code.

**If you are a new chat: this is where the last one stopped. Read all of it, do section 1, and
carry on. Do not ask the owner to repeat anything that is written here.**

**As of 26 November 2026** — portal `BUILD 2026-11-25-s` · call app `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-25-d` ·
Android app `versionCode 19` · handbook `docs/HANDOFF.md` up to **Part 164**.

---

## 1. The first five minutes of a new chat

1. `git log --oneline -8`. Anything above the newest change named in section 4 landed after this
   file was last written — by another chat, by the owner, or by Demit. Read those diffs before
   trusting the rest of this page.
2. **Take over the scheduled tasks** (section 6). The ones that fire into a chat stop reaching
   anybody when that chat ends, and nothing says so.
3. `npm ci && npm test` — green before anything is changed (about a minute).
4. To look at the live sites, the chat must be on the cloud environment named **`hope-pmo-v2`**
   (it allows the two site addresses and nothing else). A chat started on the default
   environment can move onto it; the owner approves once.
5. Then carry on with section 5, or with what the owner asks next. Report in plain words.

## 2. The owner's standing orders

They hold in every chat. Most are already written down, in the owner's own words:

- **`docs/HOPE-OWNER-HANDOFF.md` sections 2 to 4** — ship without asking ("so always, merge or
  push without prompting me"), the two things that never break, one definition of a rule, which
  source wins, how they write and how they want work delivered.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — the rules of the code. **`ARCHITECTURE.md`** — the Postgres budget and the
  hints table, both "forever" orders.

Given since, and written nowhere else:

An instruction the owner passes on from their own management is an order to carry out: answer
what was asked, first and directly; say a risk once, briefly; then do it.

## 3. What a chat can and cannot reach

- **No database.** A chat cannot run SQL or open Supabase. Migrations are run by the owner by hand
  (`db/RUN-ME-*.sql`), and every code path must work without them.
- **The live sites** only from the `hope-pmo-v2` environment (section 1).
- **GitHub**: this repository, through the GitHub tools.
- **Keys are never used, repeated or stored by a chat**, wherever it sees one. Database keys were
  shared outside Vercel in November 2026 while the new server was set up; they are due a rotation
  once the move settles (`docs/SECURITY.md` section 1). That is the owner's to do.
- **The Android app cannot be built on a chat's machine.** "Build HOPE Calls APK" in GitHub
  Actions is the only compiler: run it by hand on the branch first (it builds and keeps the file,
  and publishes nothing). Merging anything under `android/` or `app-version.json` hands the new
  app to every handset.

## 4. Where the work stands

Newest first. `#` numbers are pull requests on `samaritantechs/hope-pmo-v2`.

The Console's key was created 25 Nov and had never been used. The report has no typed question after 21:59, so it cannot say what the server sees now; the Settings card says (*AI ON, NO KEY* or *the provider key is set on the server*). **Read against the documentation, not against the provider:** the request `buildRequest` sends (model `claude-haiku-5-5`, thinking disabled, effort low, one cached system block, no sampling settings, no prefill, no tools) holds nothing the API skill's notes on Claude Haiku 5.5 list as a 400 (`budget_tokens`, non-default sampling parameters, prefill; thinking disabled is accepted at effort high or below); the provider has still not seen one. **What the report showed, said to the owner (no name is kept here):** (1) four of the nine unanswered typed questions asked for figures of the person's own or their teams' (how much is left to reach today, how are my teams doing, which team calls least: the owner's own test at 17:49, how do I know how many calls I made): leaders' ones are what the reports are for, an officer is given no report by design, and "left to reach today" is in neither report; (2) one typed question reads as a complaint about the CLEARED rule (paid customers staying on the day's list, the owner's orders of 13, 15 and 16 Nov) and may also read as "why do I see only paid customers": uncertain; - **#820 — the second reader's 24 findings on #814, put right: the free path has a pace and a ceiling, a question of megabytes and look-alike digits are handled, the bubble stops tapping through, forgets an officer who signed out and leaves the person's focus alone, and the report says what it counts (25 Nov; handbook Part 163, addendum 1; `api/_lib/claudian.js`, `claudian-match.js`, `claudian-faq.js`, `claudian-chats.js`, `public/mod/claudian.js`, `hopeai.js`, `hopeai-report.js`, `public/call.html`, `public/app.html`, `db/RUN-ME-090` (comments only), `tools/db-checks/claudian-chats.mjs`, `tools/browser-checks/claudian-bubble.mjs`, the Claudian, chat-log, tab, report and matcher tests, 34 rows of the language audit's data; `BUILD 2026-11-25-n`, `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-25-d`).** Not the owner's request: the rule of section 7 (merge first, then have it read, nothing owed). Three readers read #814 after it merged and reported **24 findings: 8 bugs, 3 privacy, 1 rule, 12 nits** (one defect found twice: 23 distinct); each was read against the code and, where a test could show it, a test now fails on the code as it was. **Server:** a lone generic word (*approve, idhini, start, mazungumzo* ...) no longer answers about something else (alone it offers the entry as a choice); a question about one's own leave balance (*do I have, balance, salio, nimebakiwa* ...) is not answered with the allowance of 26; **a typed question with the AI off now keeps the person's 2-second pace and a ceiling of 300 answers a day without the model (typed, greetings, taps), counted in memory, one row says the ceiling was met and the rest write none**; the text is cut to 4,000 characters before any regular expression runs over it (a megabyte held the process and from 6 MB threw a 500); **a number in full-width or Arabic-Indic digits, or with a zero-width space inside, is masked like a plain one**. **Bubble:** a touch tap that opens the panel no longer presses the page control under the finger afterwards (HOPE Calls' *Log out?*: the mouse events and the click within 24 pixels of the tap are swallowed for 450 ms); **signing out of HOPE Calls forgets the officer's panel, list and last exchange** (an answer still on its way is ignored); the list arriving does not take the focus from the box; the focus ring has contrast; the focus comes back to the box after a ready question pressed by keyboard; **a question that was not answered goes back into the box and a server fault is said as a fault**; SW / EN pressed while something is on its way is caught up afterwards; on a short window the panel goes beside the bubble, not over it. **Log, tab and report:** B9 is three lists (nothing came near / a ready answer was only offered / a figure of the person's own); 'ready' in the tables is said to be taps plus matches; the report and the tab's foot now say **a customer's name or a short reference typed into a question is kept as typed**; the behind-last-week line counts only chats whose standing is known; a greeting is not a chat's first question nor a need in B5; a chat's Excel names the ready question a match answered; the migration's comments and the kept PostgreSQL check name the seven kinds of row (the table has no constraint on `mode`, so `match` and `nomatch` were never refused). **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** none to the system: nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`, no read added (the ceiling and pace are memory), each browser reloads the portal page once and each handset the call page once. **Checked:** twelve new tests, `tools/browser-checks/claudian-bubble.mjs` (118 points; **without the SW / EN fix 115 pass and the 3 new points are red**) and `portal-hopeai.mjs` (49), the kept PostgreSQL check (14 of 14 breaks), **deliberate breaks: 16 of 16 (log, tab, report), 22 of 22 (server), 8 of 8 (bubble module, each red in the real browser)**, `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine:** a break runner that pointed at a removed scratch worktree and two breaks that named refactored code (rebuilt); a runner that left its copies in the repository (compared and removed); my first fix for SW / EN asked for the list again only when the button was pressed, which does nothing while something is on its way. **Not done, said plainly:** the provider has still not seen a request; **customers' names typed into a question are still logged as typed (the owner's word: masking names is unreliable, one line in the panel asking staff not to type them is the cheap cure)**; **a Swahili question about the length of maternity leave is still answered with the leave-request answer, which names the annual 26 days (the owner's word on what the helper says about other kinds of leave)**; the pace and ceiling are per server instance; nothing seen on a live phone; Android 6 and 7 web views not tried; not read by a second reader.

- **#815 — the knowledge cards' retrieval keys are widened, and the measure is a kept test (25 Nov; handbook Part 159, addendum 1; `api/_lib/claudian-cards-data.js` (the `keys:` lines of the 72 cards and a note in its header), `test/fixtures/claudian-cards-questions.json` (new), `test/claudian-cards-retrieval.test.mjs` (new), `test/claudian-cards.test.mjs` (five to twenty-four keys a card, was sixteen); data and tests only, `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** Not a new request of the owner's: the matter Part 159 and Part 163 named as next, done after #814 merged. **What it is:** six agents that saw only the cards wrote twelve questions for each card (864) and proposed 562 keys to add and 125 to drop; they were filtered by rules measured on questions NOT about the card (shape; not inside or holding another key of the card; touches at most five of the questions written for cards of OTHER AREAS and none of the 88 that are not about HOPE; no key in more than six cards; 24 keys at most): 385 passed; the agents' drops together LOSE recall, so only `omba` on the imprest request card (inside *naomba*, "please"), `kiasi` and `namba` (ordinary words) were taken off, with `chap` (inside *chapati*, replaced by `chapish`), the added `nasa` (inside *inasasishwa*) and a two-letter `hr`. **Measured with the real picker, before and after:** the 160 questions strangers wrote, with the independent judges' label: the judges' card is returned for **71 of 108 (65.7%), was 64 (59.3%)**; the 864: a card finds its own question 657 times (76.0%), was 537 (62.2%), **another card is put first 264 times, was 336**, nothing is found 54 times, was 101; the 88 not about HOPE: 21 get a card, was 25; **no card got worse (54 better, 18 the same)**; 1,243 keys, was 863. **A key that only one card has brings that card by itself** (242 of the new ones are that kind), so `test/claudian-cards-retrieval.test.mjs` holds the floors (68 of 108; 622 found, 515 first, at most 292 wrong first and 70 with nothing; at most 24 of the 88; every card finds three of its own twelve; ten real questions pinned by their exact text) and **fails on a key only one card has that touches more than 7% of all the questions in the fixtures** ("nini" would touch 12.7%, "what" and "how" 7.6%); the fixture carries no name (five made-up customer names were replaced) and fails on one. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** none for the system: data in the repository, nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`, no read, no `BUILD`; a pick costs about 0.21 ms. **Checked:** 24 deliberate breaks of the keys, the engine and the fixture in a scratch copy, each turning a test red (the first run missed 4: two generic words that a 7% guard now catches, one that was not a guarded property, and one break that did not apply because of a doubled backslash in my regex); `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 159, addendum 1):** my first filter threw out the legal area's own words (it counted other CARDS, not other AREAS); a guard against a short stem inside another word threw out Swahili's own way of building a word and cost recall (not used); my first "kept questions" were my own and some found nothing (replaced by ten real ones). **Not done, said plainly:** the picker is still words and matches a stem anywhere in a word, so `nauli` (fare) is inside *ninauliza* ("I am asking"), `imei` inside *nimeingia*, `hali` inside *tafadhali*: such a card goes only to a person who holds its screen and the model is told to use a line only if it answers (changing the rule would change the ready-answer matcher too: not tried); 37 of the 108 questions that have a card still do not find it and the judges found 20 with no card at all (what to write next is on the Hope Ai tab); the 160 are not blind (I read their misses while building the picker in Part 159, and a drop that lost one of them was not taken) and the 864 and the 88 are not independent of the keys; the provider has still not seen a request; not read by a second reader.
- **#814 — the Claudian-PMO bubble is the head alone, the typing box is there for everybody, and with the AI off a typed question is answered from the nearest ready answer (25 Nov; handbook Part 163; `public/mod/claudian.js`, `api/_lib/claudian-match.js` (new), `api/_lib/claudian-faq.js`, `api/_lib/claudian.js`, `api/_lib/claudian-chats.js`, `public/mod/hopeai.js`, `public/mod/hopeai-report.js`, `test/claudian-faq-match.test.mjs` and `test/fixtures/claudian-faq-questions.json` (new), the Claudian, chats, tab, report and UI tests, `tools/browser-checks/claudian-bubble.mjs`, `docs/DEPLOY.md`; `BUILD 2026-11-25-i`, `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-25-c`).** The owner's words are the 25 Nov row of section 2 that begins "Modify the icon". **The icon:** a bare button, the drawing cropped to the head (`viewBox="61 58 390 390"`), a `drop-shadow` that follows the head's outline, a light edge on a dark page (the module knows the page's look from the first draw and follows a theme switch), a glow for the first-sight pulse, no disc in the panel's header; `docs/brand/HOPE-Ai-icon.svg` unchanged. **The box:** always shown; it was `display:none` unless the AI was on (`aiOn = cfg.on && keyPresent()`), which the owner saw as "only opts". **The matcher:** the 26 ready answers' `keys` (Swahili and English words and phrases), the cards engine's `keyHit`/`cardScore`, weights `ln(1 + N/df)/ln(1 + N)` with an ordinary word at half and a percent sign read as a word, `MATCH_MIN` 0.95, `NEAR_MIN` 0.45, `CLEAR_RATIO` 1.25, only the entries `faqAllowed` lets the person be offered; the answer carries `mode` `match` (`id`, `q`, `answer`, `near`) or `nomatch` (`answer`, `near`); the panel names the ready question a match answers and offers the nearest as buttons. **A question that asks for the person's own figure** (a word that makes it theirs and a word that asks how many: *reached % yangu ya leo ni ngapi*, *how many calls did I make today*) is never answered from a ready answer: it is told *A ready answer cannot give a figure of yours*, the nearest are offered, and its note says `figure` (none of the 364 first direct questions has both words; 14 of the 156 near-miss ones do). **The log, the tab and the report:** the new kinds `match` and `nomatch` (a match counts with the ready answers and is a typed question; only a tap is "tapped"), a counter, a column, a filter, the note of such a row in grey, B9 (typed questions nothing fitted) and B10 (matches, with the ready question each answered) in the report. **How the keys were measured:** 364 direct, 156 near-miss, 32 uncovered and 32 off-topic questions written by four agents that had not seen the keys (kept in the fixture), tuned on the even half and the odd half kept aside, then a fresh batch of 208 direct questions (two more agents) put to the keys untouched: first draft 69.2% right and 86.8% right or listed on the half they were then tuned on, and 60.4% and 84.6% on the other half (the first thirteen entries, before any tuning); tuning half 90.7% right, 100% right or listed, 2 wrong of 182; half set aside 76.4% right, 96.7% right or listed, 5 wrong of 182; **fresh batch (the honest number) 76.9% right, 94.2% right or listed, 7 wrong of 208 (8 before one ordinary verb, kuandika, was made an ordinary word after the batch had been seen); all 572 together: 81.1% right, 96.9% right or listed, 14 wrong (2.4%)**; questions about something else answered: 1 of 156 uncovered and 2 of 156 off-topic questions (asked as the admin, a phone leader and an officer) got a ready answer: the two are the same greeting, which the server answers as small talk before the matcher, and the one is the leave answer for a question about maternity leave days; of the 260 near-miss questions 124 got the nearest ready answer (about half rightly: it defines the term that was asked about). **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no read added (arithmetic over data in the repository; one log row per typed question, as a tap already writes one); each browser reloads the portal page once and each handset the call page once. **Checked:** 14 tests of the matcher and its keys, two through the real handler, the chat reader, the tab and the report extended, two pins on the module, `tools/browser-checks/claudian-bubble.mjs` (94 points, pictures looked at: the head on light and dark pages and in the header), **67 deliberate breaks, each turning a test red** (three were missed at first and their tests strengthened (a floor on the matcher's choices, a limit on how many entries share a key, a pin that the dark theme's rule satisfied); one named a break that was no break and was dropped); `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 163):** words counted twice (a key and its plural, a stem and a longer form); topic words shared by related answers tied them; `reach` inside `reached`, `zima` inside `nzima` and a key one slip from `inalipwa` answered questions about something else; the half set aside was looked at after tuning (the fresh batch is the honest number); two of my pins did not pin (a selector that also matched the dark rule; a cap of six where the largest share was three); my first box check looked for the class the old code added. **Not done, said plainly:** it is words, not understanding (a question that asks for a person's figure finds the ready answer that defines the term, named as such); **with the AI ON a typed question still goes to the model first (using a ready answer when it clearly fits, before a token is spent, is offered)**; the keys cover the 26 ready answers, and **the 72 knowledge cards' own retrieval keys, where the independent judges' 160 questions found 40 misses and 19 partial ones, were the next matter (a data-only change): DONE in #815, above**; the Swahili of the new words is mine; nothing seen on a live phone or the live portal; no `hints` tip; not read by a second reader.
- **#813 — the Hope Ai tab: Export report saves everything on file as one text file for a chat to read, and Clear deletes exactly what it held (25 Nov; handbook Part 162; `api/_lib/claudian-chats.js`, `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `api/_lib/audit.js`, `public/mod/hopeai.js`, `public/mod/hopeai-report.js` (new), `public/app.html`, `test/claudian-chats-report.test.mjs` and `test/hopeai-report.test.mjs` (new), `test/hopeai-page.test.mjs`, `test/claudian-chats.test.mjs`, `test/pages.test.mjs`, `tools/browser-checks/portal-hopeai.mjs`, the language audit's list of model-facing files; `BUILD 2026-11-25-h`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** The owner's words are the 25 Nov row of section 2 that begins "I need to always export a report". **What it is:** a card on the tab, *Ripoti kwa Claude / Report for Claude*. **Export** asks the server for the WHOLE history (not the 31-day window: the clear must not delete what was never exported), 500 rows a page, oldest first, drawn at the newest row's own time (`upTo`), so a chat written while it is made is not in it and is not cleared with it; builds one plain-text report in the browser and saves `Hope-Ai-report-<day>.txt`. **The report** (English, written for a chat): a note to the chat that reads it, **part A the counts** (kinds, tokens, by day, role, team), **part B what to look at first**, each hint found by a plain rule it names (held rows, stops and failures in notes, **typed questions answered with no card and no report: the likeliest gaps in what Claudian knows**, answers that say it did not know, the same question typed by several people, words that look like a try at the rules, the heaviest users, the ready questions tapped), **part C every chat** with its questions, answers, cards and reports, and an END line. If fewer rows reached the page than the server counted the file says WARNING and the clear stays dead. **Clear** is the admin's alone, offered only after an export of the visit that came back whole, asks first in both languages with the counts, sends the line and the count back; the server counts up to the line and **refuses, deleting nothing, when there is MORE than the report held**, deletes a week of days at a time (stops at 30 seconds: press again), is audited, and leaves a note (`CLAUDIAN_LAST_CLEAR`) that the tab shows ("History last cleared ... by ... (N rows)") and the next report's header carries; the tab then reads the history again. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call` (tests fail on the names there; four lazy imports in `portal-core.js`); the list gained one read of one setting row through the memo; the export and the clear are an admin's, rare, page by page and week by week. **Checked:** 13 + 13 tests of the server and the text, nine of the page, **49 points in a real browser** (a real download that holds the chat of ten days ago, the clear keeping a chat written after the report, the audit line, a ticked role refused), the picture looked at; **43 deliberate breaks, each turning a test red** (one was missed at first: the test's late chat was on a day the clear never reaches); `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 162):** a test clock earlier than the seeded chats, which the server rightly refused as a line in the future (the browser check passed or failed by the hour it ran until it was given a server clock); `padStart` in a module the Android app's older web views read; a stand-in that threw where the real call rejects. **Not done, said plainly:** no Excel of the rows and nothing automatic (the owner presses the button; the clear never runs by itself); the hints of part B are words and plain rules, not understanding (B4 and B6 have false hits and misses); a reload between the export and the clear means exporting again; the order-by has no index of its own (not measured at a million rows: the owner clears regularly); a ticked role can export everything on file but never clear; not read by a second reader. **The first real use is the owner's:** press Export on the tab, send the file to a chat, and a chat reads it as section 2 says.
- **#812 — the notification bell tells today's promise again every three hours while nobody has acted on it, and a promise logged today runs too (25 Nov; handbook Part 161; `api/_lib/notify.js`, `api/_lib/call-core.js`, `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `public/call.html`, `test/bell-repeat.test.mjs`, `test/speed.test.mjs`; `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-25-b`, portal `BUILD` unchanged).** The owner's words are the 25 Nov row of section 2 that begins "At notification bell" (two messages, the second: "I expect even the todays promises that fall under that part to run too"). **What it is:** a promise due today has marks every three hours, and while it is still open the item's time is the latest mark that has come, so the same item is new again for a reader who had marked everything read: a promise logged on an earlier day starts at 09:00 East Africa time (09:00, 12:00, 15:00, 18:00, 21:00); **a promise logged today starts at the moment it was logged (the register's `comment_at`)**, new at once and every three hours after it; none after 21:00; an overdue promise is not repeated. **Who is reminded:** the register still carries the promise (a chosen follow-up status replaces it) and the customer still owes; **nobody spoke to the customer today** (the grey *Amepigiwa leo* tick, now ONE definition, `calledHit_`, asked by the lists and by the bell: any officer's call longer than the minimum seconds to the customer's own number or the guarantor's, never "Ana namba nyingine"); **no payment was typed for them today** (the Iliyonasia register, any of its four books). One function decides who is left alone for both doors (`promiseSkipsToday`), within 800 ms: **an answer that cannot come in time, a failure or a door with no check reminds nobody (the bell as it was).** The answer names `nextRepeatAt`; **the call page looks again at that mark** (`bellRemind_`: once, spread over 90 seconds, never when hidden, never an interval) and says so in a toast. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload`; a reader with no promise due today pays exactly what the bell cost (tested: the call logs and the typed payments are not read); with one, the day's called numbers and the typed payments once each behind the caches they already have (a cold book: the portal's bell 5 to 9 trips, the phone's 6 to 10, `test/speed.test.mjs`); two more columns on the promise read. **Checked:** 16 tests (marks at every boundary, a promise logged today new at once for a reader who had read everything, who is spoken to and who paid, the failure cases, both doors, the reads counted, the page's clock run on its own), one speed test; **39 deliberate breaks, each turning a test red, none missed** (the reminder never raised, four hours instead of three, marks into the night, a mark after the day's last, the first telling not at nine, a promise logged today treated as a morning one, a logged time ahead of the clock or not asked for or not handed on, an overdue or tomorrow's promise reminded, the done-with reminded, a missing, throwing or array-answering check, the check asked with nothing to ask about, a cleared customer, the guarantor's column and calls, typed payments, yesterday's, a payment netting to nothing, one book of four, the slow wait, the list's own tick, each door without the check, the earliest next mark, the reminder words; on the page: never looking again, no spread, a hidden page asking, every item a toast, two timers, a far mark, an immediate loop, the build not moved). `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 161):** the first build repeated only the morning's promises on a fixed 09/12/15/18 grid and did not make a promise logged today new when written: the owner's second message said so within the hour; a slow-read stand-in that lost its delay after the first chained call; the check's wait first copied from the report reader (1.5 s) where a bell can wait 800 ms. **Not done, said plainly:** no lock-screen alert (the app's native poller exists only for imprest alerts; a reminder with the app closed is a new Android app, handed to every handset when merged: offered); the three hours count from the first telling, not from when the officer last read it; the portal's page has no timer; the office readers see the same reminders for the teams they hold (one implementation, two doors); a payment made today that nobody typed and the evening file has not shown is not seen; not read by a second reader. **The Hope Ai export-and-clear of the same hour (section 2) is #813, Part 162, built right after.**
- **#811 — Claudian-PMO reads the reports: a typed question that needs figures ("team gani leo hawapigi simu kabisa") is answered from the Calls report and last week's results, for the person's own teams only, the lowest first (25 Nov; handbook Part 160; `api/_lib/claudian-data.js` (new), `api/_lib/claudian.js`, `api/_lib/claudian-knowledge.js`, `api/_lib/call-core.js` (one word: `scopedUser` is exported), `public/mod/hopeai.js`, `public/app.html`, `test/claudian-data.test.mjs`, `test/claudian.test.mjs`, `test/hopeai-page.test.mjs`, the language audit's list of model-facing files; `BUILD 2026-11-25-g`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** The owner's words are the 25 Nov row of section 2 that begins "i want typing". **Read as:** typing already exists (a box when the AI is ON), so what was built is the reading of reports; it is also the owner's answer ("yes") to the question that ended Part 159. **What it is:** two read-only reports worked out by the screens' OWN functions, never a second rule: **CALLS BY TEAM, today and this week** (`reportCoreForPortal`: every registered active officer, at zero when they have not rung; the teams with no call today, every team lowest first or the lowest 8 and highest 3, the officers with none) and **LAST FULL WEEK BY TEAM** (`performance_records`: lowest sales, collection and recovery in % of target, the teams below target on all three, the teams with no record). **Who:** a phone leader (both reports, for the teams their access code holds NOW, as the phone's own Ripoti reads them), a portal person with the Calls tab (calls) or any of the weekly, dashboard, performance or reports screens (results), the admin (both); **a phone officer is given none**. **When:** a word of the topic AND a word of comparison (`viewsFor`); how-to and rule questions wake nothing. **What reaches the model:** under DATA in CONTEXT, about 400 tokens at most (ninety teams stay under 1,600 characters), names cleaned to text, no phone, customer, reference or amount; the prompt says to use only DATA, never invent, say what DATA lacks and name the screen, and that a zero may be a phone not yet synced. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call` (a test fails on the name in either or in the call core); a report is read once for each set of teams and day, kept five minutes, shared while in flight, a failed one not kept, at most four seconds' wait (then the model is told it did not come); a person who asks nothing of this kind costs nothing. **The switch:** the Settings row `CLAUDIAN_DATA` (`OFF`, `NO`, `FALSE` or `0` = never read a report; no button on the Claudian card). **On the Hope Ai tab:** a grey note *Takwimu / Data: calls by team*, red with *(late)* or *(failed)*. **Checked:** 13 tests of the views, 7 through the real handler, 1 for the tab's note; **41 deliberate breaks, 40 turn a test red and one is an equivalent mutant (the `catch` around the reading cannot be reached today: `readViews` never rejects)**; `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 160):** the phone's live scope was tested on the unit only, so a break that dropped the phone's own record passed everything until a whole-path test was written; one break named an import line that does not exist. **Not done, said plainly:** the provider has still not seen a request; a person's own numbers are not reported (an officer is given none); only these two reports (others are one function, a topic line and a test each); today's calls read low early in the day until phones sync (the answer says so); not read by a second reader. **The owner's second message of the same hour is in section 2 (the Hope Ai export and clear): it is the next change.**

Exp.Def is on every officer's phone; the earners' column is role and ZONE; the Daily Bonus Plan is a proposal picture whose percentages are not in Settings; the leave allowance of 26 is the admin's to change. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no read added (data in the repository, 0.1 ms to pick); per AI question about 600 characters of cards when one matched (73% of the suite's questions did; at most 1,100 + a 120-character heading); the cached front grew by about 110 tokens; each browser reloads the portal page once. **Checked:** `test/claudian-cards.test.mjs` (the engine on made-up cards: weights, common words, slips, phrases, caps; every card whole; no developer's word or company figure; the provider has still not seen a request (no key in this session); retrieval misses questions whose words no card carries (the gaps are listed in section 8); a phone officer is not sent a card about a portal screen their own role may hold in the portal; the cards were read by a second reader, **the picker was not**.

"So after that experience too we'll be back here to check what should be considered from the chats."). **A chat is one person's day** (the rows the helper logs share a `thread`: a one-way key, a colon, the day). **The tab** (*Hope Ai*, HOPE Management, directly under Audit log; one line in `tableHtml` and one CSS rule; `S.fit = true` is exactly what it was). **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call` (tests fail on the names there; `api/upload.js` imports `portal-core.js`, so the reader is loaded by a dynamic `import()` made when the functions are CALLED: a test fails on a static import); no read added to any other screen; on the tab one request for the window (two reads through `idx_claudian_chats_day`) and one for each chat opened (`idx_claudian_chats_user`); nothing for people who do not hold the tab; three pictures looked at; the list at 1366 and 1700 px with no word broken in two)**; **63 deliberate breaks each turn a unit test red** (and the browser check goes red as well when the wrapping rule is broken); the language audit has 0 English-only rows without my suggested Swahili (17 added); `npm test` green (2,221). **Mistakes of mine (Part 158):** my first browser check read the boards' table rows as the list's and I miscounted my own seed; a drag (mouse, finger, pen; under six pixels is a tap) moves it anywhere and it stops whole at eight pixels from every edge; its place is kept as a fraction of the screen (`claudianPos.portal`, `claudianPos.call`), so a reload, a turned phone or a resized window keeps it inside; SW / EN flips the panel for the sitting and not the page; Escape closes it; a dark page gets the dark panel. **Loading and cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** the portal asks for the module seven seconds after the person is in (`claudianLater_`, `/mod/claudian.js?v=BUILD`), HOPE Calls eight seconds after the officer is in (the very last statement of `enterApp`, only if they are still in, `?v=CALL_BUILD`); a module that cannot be fetched leaves no bubble and no error; **it makes no request when mounted: the server is asked nothing until somebody taps the bubble** (counted in the browser check), then one `status` (kept a minute) and one request for each tap or question; - **#786 — legal recovery by phone: a second Legal card with the week arrows, stamped so that it outlives the call logs, and beside each lawyer on the dashboard's boards and the Presentation's Legal slide (24 Nov; handbook Part 139; `db/RUN-ME-089`, `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `api/_lib/annual.js`, `public/app.html`, `public/mod/present.js`; `BUILD 2026-11-24-a`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** The owner's words are the 23 Nov row of section 2. **What was there:** the call app already writes the customer's loan number on every call it can tie to one (their number, the guarantor's, one under "Ana namba nyingine") and a leader's phone carries its access code's name; but call logs are kept two weeks (this week and last). **The design: the database keeps the facts, the portal decides.** `db/RUN-ME-089` (pasted in the chat on 24 Nov and compared byte for byte with the file; NOT RUN when this was merged) stamps one row a customer, a phone and a day: every customer of that day's INITIAL file (the date, the date's own weekday, the newest upload of each team: RUN-ME-058's reading) SPOKEN TO (longer than `call_min_secs()`, not missed, rejected or blocked) from a phone whose role reads legal (its access code's when exactly one code carries the name, else the registered one), with the D.C and D.S that morning and what they recovered that day (initial less the later file; off it = all; no later file = nothing yet, `paired` false). The portal applies the two rules by the definitions it already has: `isLegalRole` (who is a lawyer) and `missedDc_` >= `LEGAL_MIN_DC` (who counts), so neither exists twice. **The clock:** pg_cron every ten minutes under a 20-second limit: today when nine minutes old, the three days before when eight hours old, **a day is read again only when its signature changed** (the data stamp, the minimum, the legal phones, the legal calls of the day), one never-stamped older day a tick back to the Monday of last week, **never again after three days** (so the rows outlive the call logs), one tick at a time; a stamp by hand on a day whose logs are gone keeps its rows. **The read:** `legal_phone_week(from, to, teams)`, ONE value, with its own tick (25 minutes, one day) for a server whose cron has stopped; a failing stamp never fails it. **The portal:** `legalPhoneRead_` (not run = `on: false` and the file's name, remembered five minutes; failed = `on: null`), `legalPhoneLines_` (one line a customer a day; the lawyer who spoke longest named; the day's recovery once; whose number), `legalPhoneCard_`, `legalWithPhone_` (one floor for the arrows); on `demandNotices` (`legal.phone`), on `legalRecoveries` for a week (`legal.phone`, `phoneRows`) and on `officerBoards` (`phToday`, `phWeek`, `phSpoken` on each lawyer's row; `legalPhone` with `others`). **The pages:** the Legal tab's second card, the notices card's twin (one reading, one week, either card's arrows move both; a dash and the file's name until the file is run); its list (the same drawer, kind `phone`: day, lawyer, customer, number, talk time, owed that morning and after, recovered, noticed); a "Kwa simu / By phone" column on the dashboard's two lawyers' boards with a line above them; "By phone today" and "By phone (week)" on the Presentation's Legal slide with the same line. **The two cards are NEVER added together.** **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload`; nothing on `/api/call` (not touched; a test holds that); ONE more trip on the Legal tab (8 to 9), on the week's Legal list (5 to 6, another week 4 to 5: budgets raised by one, the reason beside the rows) and on the Officer boards (27 to 28, 21 to 22); none on today's list, the dashboard's own load or the annual deck (`phone: false`); the database: a look every ten minutes, and a day's two files read only when a legal call or an upload changed it (two million deck rows in the worst order: 15,598 pages and 0.5 to 0.7 s for a day of 20,852 rows; 0 pages for a day with no legal call; 45 pages of the call logs for an unchanged day); **the live seconds are NOT known.** **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/legal-phone.mjs` (the real schema; the file as ONE text; every stamped row against a model; the clock step by step; the read; privacy; pg_cron, the job's command RUN by it; the cost in pages; **67 of 67 deliberate breaks of the file caught**), `test/legal-phone.test.mjs` (9 tests), four new tests in `test/pages.test.mjs` that RUN the builders, `tools/browser-checks/portal-legal-phone.mjs` (new: 47 points in a real browser; the three older checks of these screens still pass: 80, 52 and 24 points), **56 of 56 deliberate breaks of the server code and the pages caught**; `npm test`: 2,058 pass. **Mistakes of mine (Part 139):** one row a customer at first (a phone that only looks legal could hide a real lawyer); the first tick read the deck at every tick; **I left the date's weekday out of the deck read** and found it only through a wrong file name in a pin; two more trips than needed at first, the annual deck charged for a card it does not draw; four of the check's first breaks not caught. **Not done, said plainly:** RUN-ME-089 is not run (nothing shows and nothing is stamped until it is; nothing before the Monday of last week can ever be stamped); the live figures are not known; D.C 2 and above ONLY (Part 111's D.S 11-12 waiver not applied), LONGER than the minimum (exactly five seconds does not count), the whole day's fall in arrears (not only after the call): each the owner's to change; the lawyer credited is who spoke, whatever team the customer is on; the dashboard has no phone card; no `hints` tip; not read by a second reader; nothing seen on a live screen.
- **#785 — the demand notices issued before the system began, read off the legal team's sheet and imported by SQL: the template, the block builder, the kept check; the filled blocks pasted in the chat (built 23 Nov, its two locks and the paste 24 Nov; handbook Part 138; `db/TEMPLATE-historic-demand-notices-import.sql`, `tools/historic-notices/build_blocks.py`, `tools/db-checks/historic-notices-import.mjs`, `test/historic-notices-import.test.mjs`; no code that runs for a user, no `BUILD` change).** The owner's words are the 23 Nov row of section 2. **The sheet:** 730 rows from five officers, sorted by loan number; **258 with no REF NO left out as ordered; 472 with one.** **The dates:** 400 cells are text and every one has a day over twelve; the real date cells with a day of twelve or under were typed day first into sheets that read month first and stored exchanged (a cell says 1 December 2026: it is 12 January). Read as: T text day first (255 of the 472), C text with a slip that has one reading (4), S a date cell as stored (84), X exchanged because as stored it falls outside the sheet's period (83), **x / s both readings possible and the officer's own rows decide (36 exchanged, 7 as stored: four officers' sheets exchange, one reads right; Sundays agree the three times they can speak)**, U nobody can read it (3: the sheet's rows 254, 400, 401). **The SQL:** six data blocks fill a private list (`public.demand_import_2025`) and change no notice; the engine is ONE statement with COUNT (changes nothing, prints what an import would do) or IMPORT on its CHANGE line; a second IMPORT writes nothing; the undo is at its foot. **Two locks (24 Nov; "so that you don't import with mistakes"):** each data block carries the fingerprint of its own lines (md5 of the lines in order; blanks at the ends, Windows line ends and empty lines aside) and, with one character changed, a line missing or the paste cut, writes NOTHING and answers `DATA BLOCK n OF 6 DID NOT ARRIVE WHOLE`; a whole one answers `data block n of 6: OK` (79, 157, 233, 311, 391, 472 rows) and says when a block before it is missing; and the engine is built for the list's 472 rows and writes nothing on any other count (a half list completed later would date a default clearance by the wrong notice). **What the chat pastes is read back from the session's own record and compared byte for byte with the builder's files.** **Held by the system = a notice of that loan number within seven days.** Written: loan number, team, customer, notice date, arrears at notice, issued by, stamped at noon; no Kumb.Na. made up, no letter, **no upload stamp (a register upload never deletes them)**, the phone from the follow-up register only where the system holds the loan; the sheet's principal, total loan and total paid stay in the list (a notice has no column for them). **Cleared by default:** a customer whose loan number was never on a deck (asked through the (team, loan number) index, never read end to end), in the follow-up register or on an expected sheet (asked only when they have an index by loan number) gets ONE line in `notice_payment_ledger` marked `default`, one month after their LAST notice, for its arrears, under each of their notices with one payment id; with no arrears figure, nothing. **The screens read it with no code changed** (the past week holds the clearance, credited to TODAY's holder of the team; the Legal tab says Cleared off the deck; the arrows reach back to August 2025). **On a copy holding the system's own 1,045 notices and nobody on a deck:** 6 written twice, 28 already held, **435 notices for 416 customers, TZS 414,481,113 at notice**, 387 cleared by default (TZS 389,494,913; the live book will say fewer), **noticed customers 992 to 1,363, owed at the latest notice TZS 762,290,842 to 1,095,521,565: the weekly Rec % reads about a third lower from the day of the import, for the same money** (owed is its divisor and a cleared notice stays in it: the existing rule). **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; COUNT took 0.1 s and asked for 4 pages of a two-million-row deck table; a 45-second ceiling. **Checked:** the kept check on the real schema against a model (a block damaged in eight ways writes nothing and says so in words; the same block with Windows line ends, indented or with empty lines is the same block; the list is private on a book that hands new tables to the API's roles; COUNT changes nothing and counts right; an engine built for another number of rows writes nothing; IMPORT writes exactly the model; a second run; the fold; the undo byte for byte; both states of the expected sheets' index; **49 of 49 deliberate breaks caught, 36 of the engine and 13 of the data block**); the six real blocks and the engine run on the copy with every lock tried on them, **each of the twelve fields of all 472 rows equal to what the builder read off the workbook, and the 435 notices and 405 clearances written equal to a model written again in Python** (COUNT, IMPORT, COUNT, IMPORT, the undo); `test/historic-notices-import.test.mjs` (8 tests). **Mistakes of mine (Part 138):** taking the row order for the date order; trying to settle a date by nearby notices (it only measured that text dates have days over twelve); a redundant clause two breaks slipped through; a "pages" count that counted index visits; an empty line called indented; **the blocks were tested at 17:46 EAT on 23 Nov and I wrote the handbook before giving them: the session stopped at about 17:50 and came back at 03:22 on 24 Nov, the change uncommitted and the SQL unsent for nine and a half hours** (section 7); a deliberate break first "caught" only because the check could not build it. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing run on the live book; the 258 rows with no ref; three unreadable dates; **43 dates rest on the per-officer rule and can be wrong by weeks** (one line prints them); a customer the system held and who left is Cleared on the tab but no week is credited; no screen opened in a browser (no page changed); the fingerprint guards a block's data lines, not the SQL around them nor the engine (those are compared with the files through the session's record).
- **#784 — Reached means spoken to: a call longer than the minimum seconds, on the Presentation's calls slide, Ripoti, the Calls tab and the phone; `db/RUN-ME-088` puts the same rule into the two database functions (23 Nov; handbook Part 137; `api/_lib/call-core.js`, `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `public/app.html`, `public/mod/reports.js`, `public/mod/present.js`, `public/call.html`; `BUILD 2026-11-23-i`, `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-23-a`).** "At calls slide in presentation "REACHED %" Should be percentage of connected calls against calls not just dialled b/se some ring but dont get connected" — the owner, 23 Nov (section 2). **What it was:** a number counted as reached when one of its dials was not MISSED, REJECTED or BLOCKED; those are the phone's words for an INCOMING call nobody took, and a phone has no word for an outgoing call that only rang, so nearly every number rung counted and the figure sat near a hundred. **The rule, one definition (`outcomeOf(r, min)`):** a dial is CONNECTED only when it lasted LONGER than the setting "Minimum seconds to count a call" (`CALL_MIN_SECS`, 5 by default: the rule that already ticks a customer "Amepigiwa leo"); one that rang out, or was taken and dropped inside the minimum, is **NO ANSWER**; MISSED, REJECTED and BLOCKED stay the phone's own words; a number is reached on a day when ONE of its dials was CONNECTED (redials are one number; two short dials do not add up). `callMinSecsOf` is the one reading of the setting; every rollup is given the minimum (the phone's own day, the boards' row road, the report's row road); the portal counts nothing itself. **Changing the setting moves the tick and Reached together.** **The database (`db/RUN-ME-088`, pasted in the chat, NOT RUN):** `call_report_rollup` (RUN-ME-042) and `call_board_rollup` (RUN-ME-078) are replaced IN PLACE (same names, arguments, columns) and `call_min_secs()` reads the setting's cell; each answer says which minimum it counted by (the board: `"min"`; the report: `'MIN:5'` on its number rows; its dial rows gain NO ANSWER). **Kept under the old names on purpose, against the house rule of a new name (section 7), as RUN-ME-042 did over RUN-ME-014:** a new name meant the heavy read of the week's call rows until the file is run (the read the Postgres war removed), or a chain of two functions; the mark keeps what the rule is for. **Until the file is run: Reached and Reached % are a dash (null in the answer), the red line the screens already print names the file, the slide's title says "Reached % waits for db/RUN-ME-088", every other figure is as it was, and the call rows are NOT read to work it out.** The pages: the Reached tile and columns print a dash or nothing for "not known"; a figure that is null on EVERY row totals to blank (`allNull_` in `sumRow`, `autoTotals`, `fillPctTotals`), a field a board does not carry adds up as before; the outcome board says what CONNECTED and NO ANSWER mean; the phone's leader report the same. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload`; on `/api/call` no read added on any path a handset opens (the boot's one settings query carries one more key; the leader's report asks the same function); one read of one settings row on the report's row road, which runs only where the function is not installed; on a warmed connection the board function touches 6,810 pages after the file against 6,809 before and the report 6,103 against 6,102 (a week of 140,000 calls in a book of 800,000); the first call of a new connection reads the catalogue once (about 118 and 190 pages more); each handset reloads the call page once (`CALL_BUILD`); `test/speed.test.mjs` unchanged. **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/reached-rule.mjs` on a PostgreSQL from the whole real schema, RUN-ME-042 and 078 run first (no mark before the file; `call_min_secs()` equal to `callMinSecsOf` for 25 ways of writing the cell; 160 random books under 16 settings: the board equal to `boardRowsFromLogs` exactly and in order, the report equal to `callRollupRows` as a set, every number row marked; the file twice as one text, grants, the seven-line answer, the undo; the cost; **21 of 21 deliberate breaks of the file each make a check fail** (one, anon being let onto the board, was missed at first: the check ran each broken copy after RUN-ME-078, which had already locked the function, and now runs each on a fresh book)); `test/reached-rule.test.mjs` (new, 20 tests); `tools/browser-checks/portal-reached.mjs` (new: the real page and modules against the real `portalApi` in a real browser, 24 points in both database states, no page error); **40 of 40 deliberate breaks of the code and the pages each turn a test red** (one, the boards' row road counting by five while saying the setting's minimum, was missed at first and the test was strengthened); `npm test` 2,037 pass. On the check's made-up big book 85.7% read as reached by the rule as it was and 28.6% by the rule now; **the live figure is NOT known.** **Mistakes of mine (Part 137):** the first `call_min_secs()` read a book with no setting as two thousand million seconds (`least()` skips an empty value), found by the check's first run before the file went anywhere; I took a first-call catalogue read for a per-row cost; two test mistakes. **Found, not changed:** RUN-ME-042's function keeps a NULL team and an empty-string team as two groups where `callRollupRows` joins them (no real row has an empty-string team); the report's function matches the team list in any case and its row road exactly; the boards still ignore the category (Part 116). **Not done, said plainly:** RUN-ME-088 is not run, so the live Reached % is not known (the file's answer prints it); the phone's page was not opened in a browser (its three changed expressions are pinned and its scripts parse); nothing seen on a live screen or phone; no `hints` tip (the screens say the rule); nothing scores on Reached, so no commission or performance figure moves.
- **#783 — the day's list is the day's own morning file, found by its date: cleared customers of earlier dates are off today's lists, the phone no longer lists last week's file in the morning, and the all-time lookup is gone (23 Nov; handbook Part 136; `api/_lib/expdf.js`, `api/_lib/portal-core.js`; server only, `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** The owner's words are the 23 Nov row of section 2 ("How comes staff are seeing expected defaultes with cleared like 62561111341 who are not even in the initial file of today" ... "not just dropping but analyzing how that robust problem happens"). **How it was analyzed:** the owner pasted the sample's rows off the live book (team MASWA: an initial and two current rows a day from 15 to 18 Nov, an initial row on 19 Nov, nothing since; cycle days Sunday and Wednesday); that history, with five neighbours, was replayed through the real `expdfMine`, `expdfReport`, `expdfLeader` and `expectedDefaulters` at each stage of a day. **Three faults, one root:** (1) the all-time ghost (Part 71) listed every customer who had ever left the files, on their two cycle days, for ever, CLEARED with the last arrears still shown as owed and nothing recovered, counted in every total (in the replay a day's Rec % read 25.3 where the morning file's customers make it 37.9); (2) `expdfCore` found the day's files by WEEKDAY, so **until the day's later file was uploaded the phone and the report listed the same weekday's file of LAST WEEK** (the sample as a DEFAULTER owing 18,000, a customer who entered default on Thursday as CLEARED, a customer new on that morning's file not at all); (3) the Expected Defaulters tab, in the same gap, read a customer new on the morning file as CLEARED with everything recovered. The root: the list was never built from the day's own initial file; Parts 69 to 88 patched the hour when both files are in. **The cure:** `expdfCore` finds the day's files by DATE (two one-row reads, then only the files needed): both files = the morning file's customers, owing the later file's figure, one it does not name CLEARED with the morning's arrears recovered; the morning file only = its customers owing the morning's arrears, nobody CLEARED; a later file only = as before; neither yet = the freshest file of any earlier day, nothing recovered, `date` saying which day (the phone's strip already shows that date in place of "Leo" and says the initial list is not in). `buildRows_` is handed only the period's own files; a month keeps its first deck and reads the same freshest file (a customer of the first deck whom it does not name cleared in the month; one who left before the month is not listed); the tab reads the day the same way; `ghostRowsFor_`, its cache, its budgets and `test/ghost-lookup.test.mjs` are removed (the database function and RUN-ME-076's index stay installed, no longer asked). **What staff see:** today's list is this morning's file from the minute it is uploaded; a customer who cleared earlier is on that day's list in the report and on none of today's; counts and "owed" on a leader's list go DOWN by those customers and Rec % goes UP; the dashboard's card never counted them and does not move. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** `/api/upload` nothing; `/api/call` FEWER reads on every path and none added (the list 7 to 6 with both files, 5 with one or none; the lookup that timed out in the Postgres logs, up to 800 ms of budget, is no longer run); `test/speed.test.mjs`: every Exp.Def budget lowered by one trip, the phone's daily summary 32 to 31, and the phone's Exp.Def list given its first budget (11 trips, 200 rows); the statements keep their shape (type, the weekday of the date, the date, the team). **Checked:** `test/expdf-day-files.test.mjs` (new, 8 tests on the owner's sample: the three stages on four screens, the sample's eleven days one by one, the read counts, the month, the sort, the statement shape), the ghost and unpaired-day tests of `test/portal.test.mjs` and `test/call.test.mjs` rewritten to the rule; **all 14 new or rewritten tests fail on the code as it was; 20 of 20 deliberate breaks each turn a test red**; `npm test` 2,017 pass. **Mistakes of mine (Part 136):** the first answer named one cause and offered options where a replay was owed; my first sort test passed with the sort broken. **Found, not changed:** a row with no status word and an EMPTY arrears cell reads CLEARED (`num()` makes the empty cell 0 before `isSettled` sees it); no real deck has one; offered. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing seen on the live book or a live phone; how many cleared customers of earlier dates the live lists carried is not known until `db/RUN-ME-087` is run (read-only, pasted in the chat; `test/day-lists-size.test.mjs` pins it); the other two samples were not looked at one by one; the call app page has no note for the morning stage (the list shows with nothing recovered); RUN-ME-055, 075 and 076 are not undone.
- **#782 — when Finance closes the month, approvals close the same second on every server, and a refusal is never reported as a decision (23 Nov; handbook Part 135; `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `public/mod/advance.js`, `public/mod/leave.js`; `BUILD 2026-11-23-h`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** "From next time, when finance manager closes advance request window – even approvals should be disabled so that people cant approve when he's already proceeding with already approved requests in time" — the owner, 23 Nov (section 2). **What was already true:** Finalize on the Advance report (#588) closes THIS month for new requests AND for decisions (`advClosed_`); there is no other window in the code. **What was not, found by reading the code against the owner's words:** (1) the closed state was read through the 20-second settings memo (`SETTINGS_TTL_MS`), which a write drops only on the server instance that made it, so every other instance went on deciding, and taking requests, for up to 20 seconds after Finalize, and `advFinalize` / `advReopen`, which write the whole JSON of months back, could write one instance's older answer over a newer one; no test covered a decision on a still-pending request after Finalize; (2) the advance desk's drawer, and the leave desk's, toasted "Approved" or "Disapproved" whatever the server answered, so an approver pressing Approve on a closed month read "Approved" for a save that was refused (`updated: 0`, the reason in `skipped`), and the bulk save said "Saved: 0 approved". **Built:** `settingFresh_(db, key)` (one row by its key straight from the database, the memo told what was read, a failed read falling back to the memo); `advDecide`, `advRequest`, `advFinalize` and `advReopen` read `ADV_FINALIZED` with it, in the wave of reads they already made; both desks' drawers and bulk saves say `Haikuhifadhiwa / NOT saved:` and the server's reason, as an error, when nothing was saved, and read the screen again. **No new rule:** Finalize is still the only close. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; one more read of one row by its key on an advance decision, a new request, a Finalize and a Reopen. **Checked:** `test/portal.test.mjs` (a server whose memo says open while another has finalized: a single decision either way, a bulk tick and a new request refused with their reasons, nothing saved; the memo catches up; a reopen and a second Finalize by another instance seen at once; a reopen from an instance that had not heard keeps another month's finalize: it fails on the code as it was, `expected 0, actual 1`), `test/pages.test.mjs` (the real drawers and bulk saves of both desks RUN against refused, saved, side-noted and empty answers; the four functions pinned to the fresh read); **13 of 13 deliberate breaks each turn a test red**. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing seen live: that approvals continued after the month was closed is explained by the memo and the toast, not reproduced (if any got through for longer than a few seconds, the day and the approver's name would settle it); a desk page already open when Finalize is pressed keeps its buttons until its next press, which now says NOT saved; nothing closes approvals at a set day or hour (offered, section 8).
- **#781 — the dashboard's Legal card opens its list and steps by week, like the Legal tab's (23 Nov; handbook Part 134; `api/_lib/portal-core.js`, `public/app.html`, `public/mod/legal.js`; `BUILD 2026-11-23-g`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** "the legal recovery card at dashboard should be able to openup too - with the backward and foward arrows as usual" — the owner, 23 Nov (section 2). **What was built:** `dashboardFull` answers its `legal` as the same object the Legal tab's card reads (`legalCard_`: totals plus `today`, `mon`, `sun`, `thisWeek`, `since`), and the dashboard draws it with the one card builder inside the `<!--lgw-->` markers, so everything of Parts 128 and 131 holds there with no code of its own (tap or Enter opens the week's list; ◀ ▶ step the card alone, two minutes' memory, a late answer never painting over a press; the way home; the kept focus; the chosen week riding with the remembered screen through a quiet refresh and a table search). **The dashboard's own two rules:** the card keeps its name and, on this week, today's figure first with the week, the noticed customers and the owed beneath (on another week the week's figure is the headline); and **the week bar still moves the whole dashboard and resets the card to its week** (the three bar handlers `delete S.args.lgWeek`); the card's ceiling is the REAL this week whatever week the bar is on. - **#780 — the cure of #776 proved on every screen that loads, the Legal letterhead links that never reached their module fixed, and `wireForms()` held to what it may do by tests that RUN it (23 Nov; handbook Part 133; `public/app.html`, `test/pages.test.mjs`, `tools/browser-checks/portal-repaint-wiring.mjs`; `BUILD 2026-11-23-f`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged).** The matter #775 handed on, given to this chat as "find the safest cure and prove it on more than one screen ... do not slow any screen down, do not change what a screen shows ... break the fix on purpose ... say plainly what was not done". **What happened, said first:** while this chat read all of `wireForms()` and built the proof, the chat that wrote this file was given the same matter by the owner ("fix the handlers lost after table search") and merged the same two-line cure as #776; this chat's copy of it was dropped when its branch was restarted from `main`, and this entry is what #776 did not have. **How far the defect reached** (a real browser, the real page, the real `portalApi` over the fake database, every screen that loads, the page as it was before #776): of 48 views, 41 with a table search, **nine lost handlers from their head after ONE keystroke and 32 lost nothing** (Legal; Teams & Staff; PMO deductions; Complaints; Restructuring; HR staff; Adjust; **the Imprest request form: the role and fare boxes, the live total, Send**; Imprest approval: state chips, rate editor, phone-alert policy); #776 had seen Legal and Teams & Staff. **A repaint nobody typed:** `tipsStart()` asks for the hints 2.5 seconds after sign-in and repaints the list on screen when they arrive, so on those nine screens the buttons went dead about three seconds after the portal opened (verified on the page as it was, nobody touching it; the portal opens on the tab a person last used). Whether an earlier report of a dead button was this is not known. **`wireForms()`, all 420 lines read:** it only assigns handlers and writes what it reads off the page (`adjHint`, the imprest form's `recalc`), and starts nothing (every `srv`, `render`, `go`, `drawer` is inside a handler), so it is safe once per keystroke: 44 id lookups and eleven attribute scans. **A second defect, found by the kept check and fixed here:** the letterhead links on the Legal tab call `settingForm()`, which lives in the admin module, loaded when Settings or Teams & Staff opens and not when Legal does: in a session that opened Legal first a click threw `ReferenceError: settingForm is not defined`, repaint or not, and #776 as merged still did. The handler now asks for the module (`modLoad_('admin')`), then calls it, and says the loader's words if it cannot; they are the only place `wireForms()` calls across modules. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no read added (one keystroke makes no request: counted on all nine screens); `wireForms()` 0.15 ms on 500 rows (a repaint 76 ms), 3.0 ms on 5,000 (965 ms), 1.7 ms on 3,000 (537 to 572 ms): a third of one per cent of the repaint it joins. **Checked:** `tools/browser-checks/portal-repaint-wiring.mjs` (new, 52 points; `APP_FILE` runs it on another copy of the page): every screen that loads, one keystroke and then a sort, no handler lost; the page's own repaint; after a repaint the buttons DO their work on each of the nine screens and on Settings (no table: the other road to the same wiring); a letterhead link in a session that has not loaded the admin module; one keystroke runs `wireForms()` once and makes no request, timer or listener; every paint wires the bar and the forms once; typing keeps the box's focus and caret; **on the page before #776 it fails 32 of 52 points, on #776 as merged 3 (the letterhead links), on this change none**; `test/pages.test.mjs` four new tests beside #776's pin (the draw functions and `wireForms()` itself RUN on stand-ins; anything that starts something at wiring time fails; its names pinned; the letterhead handler), of which only the letterhead one fails on #776 as merged; **16 deliberate breaks, each turning at least one unit test red, nine of them passing #776's pin (it reads the source) and the browser check catching 13** (the table is in Part 133); `npm test` green. **Not done, said plainly:** (1) **a repaint still clears what was typed into the head** (verified before and after: the imprest request's destination and nights, the adjustment's amount, and by the three-second repaint for a form started in the first three seconds): alive again but empty; the cure is not rebuilding the head on a table-only repaint, which changes what every screen does on a repaint, so the owner's yes first (section 8); (2) `wireOfficerFind()` adds one document listener per repaint on the Calls report (counted, before and after; harmless in size, not touched); (3) HOPE Loan's screens were not run in the browser (no loan endpoint in the harness; their five wirers were read: assignments only); (4) seven views have no table search and never repaint through `paint()`; (5) nothing seen on the live screen; (6) no `hints` tip (nothing to discover). **Mistake of mine, worth keeping:** I read `git log` at the start and did not fetch `main` again until I went to push; the cure had been merged while I worked. A chat that has worked for a while fetches `main` and reads its new Parts before it commits to a Part number.

- **#774 — what each index of the deck table costs an upload chunk, measured on a scratch copy: the random uuid primary key is half of the page reads, and `db/RUN-ME-085` (time-ordered ids: offered, NOT run, the owner decides) cuts one day's two decks by 37% (21 Nov; handbook Part 127, addendum 4; a SQL file, its kept check, its pins and two kept measurement tools: no code that runs for a user, no `BUILD` bump).** "postgress war please; and that shouldnt distract efficiency of system operations" — the owner, 20 Nov (section 2); CLAUDE.md section 1: the upload never slows down. **Why:** #773 found the upload's insert at 1,279 ms a statement over its last 88 runs against 445 ms over its life, cause not known, and promised this measurement. **The scratch book** (`tools/db-checks/upload-index-cost/`, a disposable cluster of its own: 2.13 million rows of the real table laid out day after day, the live book's nine indexes, 400 MB of buffers for 975 MB, about the live ratio): a chunk of 1,000 rows is inserted as `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, WAL)`, ten of an initial deck and ten of a current deck per configuration, from a cold start; the unit is pages read from outside memory. **Results (pages read for one day's two decks):** as now 22,988 (initial 14,323, current 8,665; 168 MB of write-ahead log); **time-ordered id 14,471 (-37%; 118 MB)**; no RUN-ME-076 19,997; no RUN-ME-054 20,162; both gone (the book before 12 Nov) 17,163; time-ordered id and neither 8,563 (-63%); the primary key alone 9,325; sorting rows by team and ref before writing gains nothing (-9%). **The primary key, `id uuid default gen_random_uuid()`, puts every new row in a random leaf page of its index: about half of every chunk's reads (569 of 1,197 on an initial chunk once warm, 225 of 503 on a current one).** After it come `idx_def_snap_ref` (81% of what is left of an initial chunk), RUN-ME-076's partial index (+223 a current chunk warm, +907 on a cold first one) and the flush of the two trigram indexes' pending lists (one chunk in about fifteen reads 650 more); **with RUN-ME-054 the two are 68% of a current deck's reads: my own RUN-ME-076 was measured warm on 20 Nov (+20%) and under-measured: counted cold in pages it adds 80% to a current chunk, said plainly in Part 127.** What they buy: the ghost lookup 240 to 44 ms (3,413 runs in 19 hours), the Legal search 3.3 s to 0.2 s (16 runs): about 3 to 6 s a day of live disk against about 4 to 8 s a time-ordered id takes off; **recommended: RUN-ME-085; keep both indexes unless the owner's RUN-ME-084 shows one is read by nothing.** The scratch book's cold first chunk reads 2,000 to 2,900 pages (500 to 1,200 once warm) and the live statement read 1,400 to 4,300: the same order, so at 0.5 to 1 ms a page it is the 1.3 s (an inference: this machine's cache holds the whole book). **Built: `db/RUN-ME-085-time-ordered-ids-for-the-deck-table.sql`** (pasted in the chat; five statements: `set local lock_timeout = '500ms'`, the function `public.hope_uuid_time_ordered()` (a UUID version 7: the 48-bit millisecond clock, version 7, 74 random bits, volatile), a grant to PUBLIC, `alter table public.defaulter_snapshots alter column id set default ...`, and a select that answers the new default). **No row is rewritten; the app sends no id (the deck importer sets none and the upload is a plain insert); ordering by id, the pager's tie-break, stays a total order. Its cost: the ALTER takes the table's heaviest lock for an instant, so it waits at most 500 ms and then changes NOTHING (the editor runs a script as one transaction); run it in a quiet minute, not during an upload, and again if it says "lock timeout"; PostgREST reloads its schema in the background (about 0.2 s). Undo, one line: `alter table public.defaulter_snapshots alter column id set default gen_random_uuid();`.** Checked: `tools/db-checks/time-ordered-ids.mjs` (the whole real schema, a book that takes new functions from PUBLIC, a plain role as the API's: ids version 7 and never going back, 200,000 all different, the API role writing 5,000 rows by default as PostgREST does, old rows byte for byte, the lock timeout in 0.5 s with nothing changed, again, the undo, **9 of 9 deliberate breaks each caught for their own reason**), `test/time-ordered-ids.test.mjs` (6 pins); `npm test` green. **Mistakes of mine (Part 127):** an explicit begin and commit no earlier file used (taken out); a lock-down fixture that could only add to PUBLIC's rights, so one break was caught for the wrong reason; a break that changed the undo line in the comment; a reader's backend that went on holding its lock after its client was killed. **Cost (CLAUDE.md section 1):** words, one SQL file offered, two measurement tools: nothing in the app changes; `/api/upload` and `/api/call` untouched. **Not done, said plainly:** RUN-ME-085 not run and not decided; the saving in live seconds is NOT known; `repayment_snapshots` (the Expected sheet) has the same random key and the same remedy, unmeasured and not offered; `call_logs` takes the app's own hashed id; no index dropped or proposed to be dropped (`idx_def_snap_ref` and the two new ones wait for RUN-ME-084); the 385 quick failures are still not named.
- **#773 — the owner's second RUN-ME-080 table (Saturday 21 Nov, about 15:09) read against Friday's two: the temp files are named, the upload's insert is three times its own average, and `db/RUN-ME-084` (read-only, no lock) is handed over to say why (21 Nov; handbook Part 127, addendum 3; a read-only SQL file, its kept check and pins: no code that runs for a user, no `BUILD` bump).** "postgress war please; and that shouldnt distract efficiency of system operations" — the owner, 20 Nov (section 2); the table was the answer to the ask of #772 (section 8) and came with no words. **The window is 19 h 23 min after the 19:46 table** (a Friday night and Saturday's working hours); every figure is a difference between two tables of `pg_stat_statements` (lifetime counters, statements that FINISHED only). **Good:** the old week read did not run once (122,556 in all three tables, Saturday's working hours among them: the proof addendum 1 asked for); the ghost lookup ran 3,413 times at 44 ms each, its worst its own 800 ms limit; `expected_phone_window` ran 460 times in 19 hours (150,051 in all); the phones' insert into `call_logs` 24 ms (16 over its life), worst unchanged at 1,761 ms. **The temp files, named:** about 12.5 GB (plus or minus 3) in the window from `call_board_rollup` (7.4 GB: 124 runs at 3.7 s, 60 MB of temp each, worst 6.6 s: the largest writer in working hours and the slowest statement that repeats), `recovery_standing` (about 3 GB; 3,839 runs at 424 ms against 231 over its life) and the two reads of `defaulter_snapshots` (about 2 GB); the recorder's 29 GB in about the same hours leaves 14 to 16 GB that is the night's one 12 MB file a minute, **not in this table and not named** (a spill that size for weeks would head the ranking, so it is recent or not a statement this list holds; harmless: 0.4 MB a second). **The sacred-path number to watch: the upload's insert into `defaulter_snapshots`, 88 statements in the window at 1,279 ms each against 445 ms over the 8,590 before (worst 5,279 to 5,470 ms; 11 to 34 MB read from the disk a statement); a statement carries up to 1,000 rows, so a 10,000-row deck spends about 13 s there, inside the host's 60 seconds, and nothing in the table says an upload failed. Why is NOT known.** The table carries about nine indexes and each is written for every row: the primary key (a random uuid), the two keyed by loan number, RUN-ME-076's partial index (mine: measured warm at +20%, never cold), the two trigram indexes of RUN-ME-054 (never measured for the insert) and three that follow the date; other candidates are a different mix of uploads, the machine's memory (512 MB of buffers, a table of 1.98 GB with its indexes) and the call app's load. **Second number: the phone index's read of an Expected sheet, 34 runs at 1.18 s against 228 ms over its life** (88 MB from the disk each; `fetchAll` pages of 10,000 rows re-running `ORDER BY id ... OFFSET` over the whole day: the shape the `call_logs` read had before RUN-ME-078); it is the call path's own build, so it is the next matter after the upload's insert. **The Legal search is fast** (16 runs at about 210 ms: the 3.3 and 4.1 s of #771 are lifetime figures from before 12 Nov). **Two corrections (Part 127, addendum 3):** a 5-second sampler sees a cancelled statement once, at a random moment between 3 and 8 seconds, so #772's "nothing reached the limit" proves the Friday-midday timeouts are gone and cannot prove that none was ever cancelled (the Postgres log's error lines settle it); and #771's "whoever searches by phone waits 3 to 4 seconds" was read from a lifetime average. **Built: `db/RUN-ME-084-write-path-tables-and-indexes-read-only.sql`** (pasted in the chat; one statement;
