  upload of three (the Initial deck: **8,296** rows put right by 060, the figure measured from the real file on 16 Nov, **1,636 Last trans dates impossible as stored**, 3,791 exchanged) and left alone the two Current uploads read right (**0 witnesses in 20,570 rows**, 7,325 ambiguous dates untouched);
  the second run changed 0 and the witness count stayed 1,636 (the proof is read from the date as uploaded). **065:** the payments table is empty, nothing to repair. **064:** 136 date columns, 11 flagged, 0 old; read in Part 97: not faults (the 060 log's old values, `team_holders.to_date` = `9999-12-31`, the hidden Follow-up rows),
  still the old reader's and waiting for the repairs (the decks' `disb_date` 21,756 and `last_trans_date` 41,176: the 060 log held 66,531 rows, so **two more runs for 10 to 15 Nov**), the Expected sheet's (23,909 / 28,166: only the customer page reads them; no repair), and **new: `demand_notices.notice_date` (12) and `created_at` (10), `pmo_deductions.discovery_date` (2)**.
  **RUN-ME-067 (read-only):** a notice's number `HMCL/<initials>/<dd>/<mm>/<yyyy>` carries its own day (made by the app and by the sheet's script; TEXT, so the date bug never touched it); the probe counts, for the imported (846, uploaded 12 Oct after the fresh start) and the app-made (248) notices separately,
  the notice dates that are the number's day, **the number's day with day and month exchanged**, or neither, the stamp against the number, and the deductions whose discovery date is out of order and **put in order by the exchange**; counts, dates and eight characters of an id only. **Cost:** 0.26 s for 5,000 notices (the live book has 1,094), 3.6 s at a hundred times the live size (a first form took 10 s per 5,000 notices:
  inlined steps repeated the pattern match about fifty times a row; every step is now `MATERIALIZED`). **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/notice-dates-audit.mjs` (every printed line equals a model; byte-identical afterwards; **26 of 26 breaks**), `test/notice-dates-audit.test.mjs` (9 tests, 16 breaks of the pins caught); `npm test`: 1,692 pass; no code that runs for a user changed, no `BUILD` bump.
  **Not done, said plainly:** no repair of notices or deductions (the answer decides); the Expected sheet and the decks' `due_date`; the 1 to 9 Nov window. **Two mistakes of mine in Part 97:** the check's must-fail breaks never ran, and three breaks were not breaks.
- **#728 — hardening of #727, found by reading it as an upload would meet it (handbook Part 96).** (1) `unreadableDatesOf` ran in the upload request with nothing around it: a count is housekeeping, so it now sits in a `try` and can never fail the
  write that follows (CLAUDE.md section 1: nothing new in the upload request that is not the write). (2) The page's Done handler threw on an answer with a count and no examples, **turning a finished upload into "Failed: TypeError"**: the browser check's
  fifth case reproduces it on the page as merged and passes on the fix. Also pinned: thirteen hostile cells of 100,000 characters take about 15 ms through the three date readers (no backtracking). `npm test`: 1,683 pass; no SQL, no `BUILD` change.
- **#727 — dates, matter 4b: a date no reader could read is counted out loud (handbook Part 96, `api/_lib/importers.js`, `api/upload.js`, `public/upload.html`, 17 Nov).** The owner's order in section 2 ("... different excels might
  come with different fomarts am not sure"). **What was wrong:** a cell that held something and did not read as a date was stored as an empty date (or the moment of the upload) and the screen said nothing. **Now**
  `unreadableDatesOf(type, rows, { stage })` counts them per upload, with up to three distinct examples (cut at thirty characters), from **the same column lists and the same reader the importers use** (not counted: empty
  cells and the deliberate "no date" words: a dash, N/A, nil, none, null, TBD, hakuna, 0, No Due Date; and whether a cell reads never depends on the file's order, so no order is asked). Covered: decks, the four Expected
  sheets, Followup, payments, approved loans (not the application stages), complaints, restructures, call users, call history, demand notices. `/api/upload` answers `unreadableDates: { count, examples }` as a plain number on every slice (0 when all
  read; nothing on other types) and **the page adds the slices up and says it once, in Swahili and English, from the file's total** ("N date(s) in this file could NOT be read (for example ...) ... no date was guessed"); a clean file says
  nothing. **Cost:** CPU only, no read or write added (a test counts the trips: the same with and without unreadable dates); `/api/call` untouched; the page is its own no-cache page (`BUILD`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged). **Checked:**
  `test/unreadable-dates.test.mjs` (11 tests, including **parity with the importers over 3,000 generated deck rows** and over Expected, Followup, payments and loans), 4 more in `test/upload-speed.test.mjs` (the real handler and the page's
  real `sendInSlices_`), **25 of 25 deliberate breaks caught**, and `tools/browser-checks/upload-unreadable-dates.mjs` in a real browser (12 points; **fails 6 on the page as it was**); `npm test`: 1,682 pass. **Not done, said plainly:**
  comments' promise dates, the staff registry, deductions and advances are not counted; a cell of a row the importer drops is still counted; the examples are the cells' own text; the company forms' day-first fallback (**4c**) waits
  for one real file of each (section 8); nothing to run on the live book.
- **#726 — dates, matter 4a: the formats a spreadsheet writes a date in (handbook Part 95, `api/_lib/parse.js`, 17 Nov).** The owner's order in section 2 ("... different excels might come with different fomarts am
  not sure"). **What was wrong:** two forms were read (ISO, `a/b/yyyy`) and every other form came back as nothing, in silence: month names (`09-Jul-2026`, `9 July 2026`, `Jul 9, 2026`, English and Swahili), dotted
  (`09.07.2026`), year first (`2026/07/09`), compact (`20260709`), Excel serial numbers (`46210`), a slash date with a clock. **Two hazards found beside them:** a date that does not exist was returned as one (`31/02/2026`
  became the text `2026-02-31`, which the database refuses: the whole slice of an upload fails; and the stamp reader rolled it into **3 March**). **Now** `moreDateParts_` reads the new forms in ONE place, after the two old forms,
  for the date reader and the stamp reader alike; `dayMonthOf_` is the one definition of which of a pair is the month (old forms and new); `validDate_` makes a date exist; dotted dates count as evidence of the file's order;
  and the company sheets' `dateCell_` (which cut a cell at its first space) reads a date written with spaces whole. **Whatever the old readers read is read exactly as before** (the old code is kept in the test as a
  yardstick, 60,000 generated strings compared; the only difference is a date that does not exist). **Choices written down:** an Excel serial is a date only from 2010 to 2040, and a fraction is a clock only with four digits
  or more, so an amount in a misplaced column (`45000.00`) is never a date (`46210.5` is therefore not read). **Cost:** CPU only, +0.05 microseconds per date cell (a deck's 50,000 cells: about 2.5 ms); no read or write added;
  `/api/call` untouched; no front-end file (`BUILD`, `CALL_BUILD` unchanged). **Checked:** `test/date-formats.test.mjs` (16 tests; 20,000 generated dates in every form round-trip; every serial of 2010 to 2040) and **20 of 20
  deliberate breaks of `parse.js` caught**; `npm test`: 1,667 pass. **Not done, said plainly:** a date that still cannot be read is still stored empty without a word (**matter 4b, next**: the Upload screen says how many
  and gives an example, a per-slice number the page adds up like `receivedAbnormal`); the company forms' day-first fallback on a small file with no evidence (**4c**: needs one real file of each, section 8); `July 2026`
  with no day stays unreadable on purpose; nothing to run on the live book.
- **#725 — dates, matter 3: the decks' Last trans dates (handbook Part 94, `db/RUN-ME-066-repair-swapped-last-trans-dates.sql`, pasted in full in the chat, 17 Nov).** The owner's order in section 2
  ("... we have last trans date column ..."). **What was wrong:** the old defaulters reader swapped LAST TRANS DATE like the other date columns, and `db/RUN-ME-060` never touched it (no pattern to check it
  against). **The repair is run AFTER RUN-ME-060, for the same days, and changes an upload only when the upload itself proves it TWICE:** (1) RUN-ME-060 put right at least three of its rows and they are at least half of
  its rows that fit the pattern (read from `defaulter_date_repair_log`; the half keeps a small slice the new per-request reader could not decide from making a whole upload "swapped"); (2) at least three of its rows carry a
  Last trans date that is impossible as stored (before the Disb date or after the deck's day) and possible with day and month exchanged (the column itself shows it). **A row** is changed only when its loan's dates fit the
  pattern, its date has another reading, that reading lies between the Disb date and the deck's day, and it was not changed before; rows with no upload number are never changed. **The proof is read from the date as it was
  uploaded**, so a run that stops at the row limit does not take the proof away from the rest of its upload. **It says what it left alone:** nine counting lines and one line per upload with its verdict. Log
  `defaulter_last_trans_repair_log`, undo at the foot, 3 s lock / 50 s limit, and a plain "Run db/RUN-ME-060 first" when 060's log is missing. **Cost, measured on 2.33 million deck rows with the live table's indexes:** the
  six days 10 to 15 Nov (129,600 rows) are done in two runs, 4.2 to 4.6 s for 50,000 rows and 1.0 s for the rest; every row of the six days ends with the real date and the other 2.2 million rows have the same checksum
  before and after; nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged. **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/last-trans-repair.mjs` on the whole real schema plus the real RUN-ME-060 (14,924 deck rows,
  37 uploads, 25 swapped; uploads made to stand on each line of the rule: exactly three repaired rows, two, a swapped slice in a right upload, no exchange shown, no upload number, one changed across runs): every row equals a
  model written again after every run, the verdict of every upload equals the model's, the undo is byte for byte, small runs end where one big run ends, a narrow window and a book without 060 behave, and **28 of 28
  deliberate breaks change the outcome**; `test/last-trans-repair.test.mjs` (12 tests). `npm test`: 1,651 pass. **Two mistakes of mine, in Part 94:** the first fixture's "loans whose three dates can all be misread" cannot
  exist (the chronic date is 14 days after the expiry), so half its checks passed on nothing (section 7); the first rule proved an upload by three repaired rows alone. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing run on the live book;
  **`due_date` of the decks** (the importer's fifth date column) is swapped the same way and not repaired (RUN-ME-064 shows whether any row holds one; matter 4); **the Expected sheet's `last_trans_date`** (the customer page's
  "Last payment" reads it first) has no witness and is not repaired; an upload too small to show the exchange is left alone and listed.
- **#724 — dates, matter 2: received payments' transaction dates: the reader, and `db/RUN-ME-065` (handbook Part 93, pasted in full in the chat, 17 Nov).** The owner's order in section 2
  ("... we have transactions dates at received ..."). **What was wrong (in the code; the live book not seen):** `importReceivedPayments` decided day or month from the file's own payment dates
  only, so a payments file of one day from the 1st to the 12th of a month (every value `11/10/2026`) had no evidence and **read day-first: 10 November stored as 11 October; 12 November as 11
  December, in the future.** **The reader:** `paymentsDateOrder` asks the file's evidence, then the tightest stretch of days, then the day it is uploaded (the page's `meta.uploadDate`, else the
  server's clock in East Africa: `uploadDayKey_`, one definition shared with the approved loans), and the upload's answer says which rule decided; arithmetic only, no read or write added.
  **The repair:** the upload's own time is the witness: a row is repaired when its stored date is outside `[upload day - 14, upload day + 1]` and the other reading is inside `[upload day - 7, upload day]`,
  and only in an upload (the rows of one minute) with no day over twelve in it, no ambiguous row that fits as stored, and at least one such row; the copies in `notice_payment_ledger` are put right by the
  payment's id (skipped on a book without that table); every row is in `payment_date_repair_log`, the undo is at the foot, a second run changes nothing; **the one case it cannot tell apart is a file uploaded a
  month late whose dates land on the upload day the other way round.** **Cost:** a few thousand rows (two weeks of payments), a 3 s lock and a 50 s statement limit; nothing on `/api/call`; on `/api/upload`
  arithmetic only. **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/payment-date-repair.mjs` on the whole real schema, 475 payments in 55 uploads, row by row against a model of the rule, the undo byte for byte, a second run, a book
  without the ledger, eighteen deliberate breaks; `test/payment-date-repair.test.mjs` (9 tests, seventeen breaks of the pins) and four tests of the reader. `npm test`: 1,639 pass. `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged.
  **Not done:** nothing run on the live book; the company's forms' same last resort and `dateOrNull`'s formats (matter 4); the Last trans dates (matter 3).
- **#723 — dates, everything that is wrong, one matter at a time: the order, and matter 1: every date in the book (handbook Part 92,
  `db/RUN-ME-064-every-date-in-the-book-read-only.sql`, pasted in full in the chat, 17 Nov).** "Repair everything that's wrong brother I can't be
  perfect to detect every where dates are wrong ..." (section 2, the owner's words in full). **The order:** (1) find where, anywhere (this change); (2) received
  payments' transaction dates; (3) Last trans dates; (4) every other date and the formats of different Excel files; (5) Disb dates finished (the RUN-ME-060 runs).
  **Found in the code while planning, not yet fixed (each is a matter above; Part 92):** `importReceivedPayments` reads the day/month order from that file's own
  payment dates only, with none of the two fallbacks the approved-loans importer has (tightest span, nearest the upload day), so a payments file whose dates all have
  both parts twelve or under (every file of a day from the 1st to the 12th) **reads day-first: 10 November stored as 11 October** (`paid_at` is a date; `created_at`,
  the moment of the upload, is the witness); the company's own forms have the same last-resort day-first on a small file; and **`dateOrNull` reads ISO and `a/b/yyyy`
  only: `09-Jul-2026`, `09.07.2026`, `2026/07/09`, `20260709` and an Excel serial number read as nothing, and the upload does not say so.** **Matter 1:** the scan finds every
  date and timestamp column of every base table of the `public` schema from the catalogue (it names no table: a table made by a migration is in it), reads each table once, and prints per
  column the rows, filled, earliest, latest, later than today (the East Africa date; a day ahead of now for a moment) and before 2020; `FUTURE?` where a later date is not normal,
  `OLD?` for a date before 2020 outside a birth date, and no flag where a later date is normal (an expiry, a promise, a due date, a schedule, a start or end, tomorrow's sheet).
  **Cost:** 5.4 s on a book of the live book's size, one process, a 45-second ceiling, nothing written; nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no code that runs for a user changed.
  **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/every-date-audit.mjs` runs the WHOLE of `db/schema.sql` (82 date columns) and compares every column's line with a direct query on that column, the
  flags, the order, the ANSWER and the East Africa clock, with seventeen deliberate breaks; `test/every-date-audit.test.mjs` (6 tests, eleven breaks of the pins, each caught). `npm test`: 1,626 pass.
  **Not done, said plainly:** nothing run on the live book; the matters after the first.
- **#722 — where a Disb date later than today still lives: every table that holds one (handbook Part 91, second addendum,
  `db/RUN-ME-063-future-disb-dates-everywhere-read-only.sql`, pasted in full in the chat, 17 Nov).** "I mean there could be future dates e.g
  into December 2026 than today's 17th Nov 2026" — the owner, 17 Nov, after #721. **RUN-ME-062 had made exactly that test, on one table:**
  the Defaulters Followup tab has none (its latest Disb date is 9 Nov). A Disb date is stored in FOUR tables (`followup_status`,
  `defaulter_snapshots`, `repayment_snapshots`, `loans`: pinned by a test against `db/schema.sql`), and the old importer's swap went into every one
  of them from early August to 16 Nov. The file counts the later-than-today dates in each, **week by week for the decks** (how far back RUN-ME-060
  still has to reach), and **customers whose last-known deck row has a Disb date later than today and is older than the latest current deck**:
  the December dates a CLEARED customer shows on Expected Defaulters (the newest current row of the customer, the rule of Part 71 and RUN-ME-055,
  pinned), each also placed on the wrong cycle day and leader. **A clean answer is not proof** (a swap reaches the future only for the 12th of a month; the
  pattern tests are RUN-ME-061 and RUN-ME-062). **Cost:** a read of the decks table end to end (2.3 million rows, 677 MB on the test machine:
  1.4 s with 28,750 later-dated rows), one process, a 45-second ceiling, nothing written; nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no code
  that runs for a user changed (`BUILD`/`CALL_BUILD` unchanged). **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/future-disb-dates-audit.mjs` on a PostgreSQL with
  the five tables, the enum and the indexes made from the REAL `db/schema.sql` blocks, a swapped book, a clean book and five clean books with one later
  date in one table, every line equal to a count made in JavaScript, twenty-two deliberate breaks each changing the answer; `test/future-disb-dates-audit.test.mjs`
  (9 tests, fourteen breaks of the pins, each caught). `npm test`: 1,620 pass. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing run on the live book; **no repair of the last-known rows
  built** (the numbers decide its shape: RUN-ME-060's rule applied to each such customer's newest current row, a few tens of thousands of rows where whole
  days are two million; section 8, offered).
- **#721 — the live answers of 17 Nov, recorded (docs only; handbook Part 91, addendum): the Follow-up list is ALL RIGHT, and the
  10 to 15 Nov repair is under way.** The owner ran `db/RUN-ME-062`: **"no Disb date is later than today and all 10,455 dates tested
  fit their own DAYS ELAPSED -- the column is all right"**: 10,455 customers on the tab, all with a Disb date (2024-04-17 to 2026-11-09),
  0 later than today, Last trans 0 of 10,401 later than today, 10,455 of 10,455 fit as stored, 0 swapped, 0 neither, 0 not tested, and
  **every row stamped with the deck of 2026-11-17**: that day's Current deck was uploaded after #711 and #717 and rewrote the whole
  list, so it healed by itself (the owner's "i hope the system autoupdates whats right"). **#720's prediction was wrong:** it said "NOT
  all right" until the next Current upload; the upload had already happened, and a chat does not know which uploads the owner has done
  since: it reads the stamps (`deck of ...` lines) before predicting. **64 rows the tab does not list** (no status, no arrears) carry a
  Disb date later than today: customers who left the book and keep the date their last write held (the old importer's swap, written
  before the fix); no list shows them, and a Current deck that names the customer again rewrites them. The same run of the owner's
  also ran **`db/RUN-ME-060` for 10 to 15 Nov** (window `2026-11-10` to `2026-11-15`, `max_rows` 50,000): 110,328 not fitting, 110,328
  with one fitting reading, **50,000 repaired, 60,328 still to do, 0 left alone** (section 8). **Found while checking who reads the
  register's date, and not asked:** Parts 85 and 86 and #712 say no screen reads the Expected sheet's `repayment_snapshots` dates (Part 85 had looked
  for a select that NAMES them; the reader uses `select('*')`); that is wrong, and both Parts are corrected in place: the customer page (`public/mkopo.html`, `api_customerLookup`) shows **Last payment** from the customer's newest Expected
  row first and from the Follow-up row second, and returns the Disb date the page does not draw. Rows stored before 16 Nov carry the old
  swap and nothing repairs them; not measured on the live book (section 8, offered). The other readers of the whole register row do
  not reach a hidden row: the phone's two reads do not select the date and drop stubs; `/api/followup` (GET) has no caller in any page,
  module or app source; the Promise to Pay tab returns the row but draws no Disb date. **Cost:** none: words only, `BUILD` and
  `CALL_BUILD` unchanged. **Not done:** nothing measured on the live book beyond what the owner pasted.
- **#720 — the Follow-up list's Disb dates: is any later than today, and does each fit its own DAYS ELAPSED? A read-only check
  (handbook Part 91, `db/RUN-ME-062-followup-list-dates-read-only.sql`, pasted in full in the chat).** "In the defaulter followup
  nav, try check if there exists any future date than today in the disb date column to confirm all dates are okay" — the owner,
  16 Nov, with the corrected RUN-ME-061 answer pasted above it. **What that answer showed:** the 16 Nov uploads all fit as stored
  (RUN-ME-060 worked); the current decks of 10 to 15 Nov and the initial decks of 11, 12 and 13 Nov still read about 20% fit as
  stored and 80% fit only if swapped (`neither` and `missing` 0, so RUN-ME-060 can put every row right); and **no upload has a
  date-like D.S**, which corrects #718's guess: the five initial decks that read right were NOT CSVs chosen on the old page (their
  D.S is intact): Part 90 and the entry below are corrected. **Why the list matters:** `followup_status` is a COPY, written by a
  Current deck (and the Followup sheet) as the deck was read at upload; RUN-ME-060 repairs `defaulter_snapshots` and does not
  name it, so until a Current deck is uploaded after the fix (#711, 16 Nov) it still holds what the old importer stored. **The
  check:** test 1 (what was asked) counts Disb dates later than today (East Africa date) among the customers the tab lists, with
  the earliest and latest, how many a swap of day and month explains, and eight examples (a loan number and a team, no name); the
  same for Last trans; **test 2 is the one that confirms**, because a swapped 2026 date lands in the future only when its real day
  is the 12th and no 2024 or 2025 date ever does: each row's Disb date, the day of the deck that wrote it (`deck_date`) and the
  loan system's DAYS ELAPSED must agree (0 to 3, RUN-ME-060's window): fit as stored / only with day and month exchanged /
  neither, and by the day of the deck. The answer line says "all right" only when test 1 is empty AND test 2 found no swapped
  date. `deck_date` is read through `to_jsonb`, so the file runs with and without that optional migration. **Cost:** a read-only
  statement, about two seconds for 60,000 rows, a 30-second ceiling; no code that runs for a user changed (`BUILD` and
  `CALL_BUILD` unchanged); nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`. **Checked:** `tools/db-checks/followup-dates-audit.mjs` on a
  PostgreSQL, the table made from the REAL `create table` blocks of `db/schema.sql` and the real migration (section 7: a fixture
  from the book's own types), four registers, every printed line equal to a count made in JavaScript, no name printed, the book
  byte-identical afterwards, seventeen deliberate breaks of the file each changing the answer; `test/followup-dates-audit.test.mjs`
  (8 tests, eleven breaks of it, each caught). `npm test`: 1,611 pass. **Not done, said plainly:** nothing run on the live book;
  **the register is not repaired:** the next Current upload rewrites every customer on it with dates read right (nothing to run), and
  a repair in place from the repaired deck rows was not built (the working day, the hottest table, hours of difference; the owner's to
  ask for) **(Answered 17 Nov, #721: the list had already healed by itself.)**; Last trans cannot be repaired by a pattern; 10 to 15 Nov in the decks wait for RUN-ME-060 with its window changed (section 8).
- **#719 — RUN-ME-061 failed on the live book: `upload_batch` is a uuid; the kept check now builds its table from the real
  schema's types (handbook Part 90, addendum).** The owner ran `db/RUN-ME-060` for 16 Nov that evening: **16,531 rows put right,
  0 still to do, 0 left alone** (the day's initial and current decks; 2209966593 now falls on Monday). Then `db/RUN-ME-061`
  stopped: `invalid input syntax for type uuid: "legacy"`. The query coalesced `upload_batch` with the text `'legacy'`, and on
  the real table it is a uuid (`db/schema.sql`); `tools/db-checks/deck-upload-audit.mjs` had declared it `text`, so a check that
  was run, timed and broken on purpose still passed on a table that is not the book's. **Fixed:** `coalesce(upload_batch::text,
  'legacy')`; the check's table now carries the real types (a uuid for every upload and an upload with no batch at all, shown
  as `legacy`), and the old query now fails on it with the owner's own words; `test/deck-upload-audit.test.mjs` compares the
  fixture's type of every column the query reads with `db/schema.sql`; four deliberate breaks, each caught. The corrected query
  was pasted in full to the owner at once. **Cost:** none: a read-only query, no code that runs for a user changed, no `BUILD`
  bump, nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`. `npm test`: 1,603 pass. **The lesson is in section 7.** Not done: nothing run on
  the live book from here; the days before 16 Nov are still swapped until `db/RUN-ME-060` is run for them (section 8).

  per upload of the last seven days: rows, fit as stored, fit only if swapped, neither, missing, and the shapes of D.S;

  deck-upload-audit.mjs` compares every line with a count made in JavaScript and shows the book is unchanged) and
  `tools/file-date-audit.mjs` (the per-file check, needs SheetJS 0.18.5: the top of the file says how). **Cost:** none
  to the system: no code that runs for a user changed, no `BUILD` bump, nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`.
  **Checked:** 9 tests of the query and the tool; sixteen deliberate breaks, each caught; `npm test`: 1,602 pass.
  **Not done, said plainly:** no real file of Expected, Followup, Received payments, Comments, Complaints,
  Restructures, Notices, Deductions, Staff, Advances or the call-app sheets was available, so none was checked against a
  real file (the Followup sheet pools loan-system columns with staff-typed ones: the one case pooling could misread, not
  seen, not changed); a spoilt D.S is not repaired (the call app shows it as `7/12`); nothing run on the live book.
- **#717 — every upload reads its dates by its own file's order, and the browser reads none of its own
  (handbook Part 89).** "I can't see 2209966593 on cleared yet., just study the dates if the position of
  month starts then day or vice versa trim the same for the rest not to stay contradicted.. since that I
  think affects multiple lists in the system so pass through how you cumpute uploaded dates bro" — the
  owner, 16 Nov. **The customer:** a chat cannot see the live book, so the answer was the read-only probe
  (`db/TEMPLATE-where-is-this-customer-read-only.sql`, filled in for the loan and the day and pasted in
  full in the chat; its header is now brought up to Parts 87 and 88): it is CLEARED on a day's list only
  when its stored disbursement date is right (RUN-ME-060), it is on that day's initial file that counts,
  and that day's current file is filed. Which of the three was not known. **The pass:** every
  `dateOrNull` and `stampOrNull` under `api/` was listed and the Upload page's own reading was measured.
  Found: (1) four sheets that read slash dates with no order (complaints, restructures, call-app
  registrations, call history; Part 85 had named the first two and left them); (2) the same four turned a
  stamp with a clock into the moment of the upload, silently; (3) **the Upload page's own reader (SheetJS,
  `cellDates: true`) decided a CSV's dates itself, month first, before the server's rule saw them:** in a
  day-first CSV `9/7/2026` went on as the seventh of September while `16/11/2026` stayed text (one file, two
  orders), `3/6` in D.S became the sixth of March 2001, an account lost its leading zero, a twenty-digit
  number its last digits (seen in a real browser with the real reader). **Now:** the four pool their own date
  columns through `inferDayFirst` and drop the clock first (`dateCell_`), reading exactly as before where a
  file shows no clue; `readWorkbook` reads a `.csv` with `raw: true`, so a CSV goes to the server as the text
  it holds and the server's rule is the only one (an Excel file is read as it was; `public/upload.html`, no
  `BUILD` bump: its own page, served no-cache). **Cost:** CPU only, no read or write added; the four
  uploads cost the same trips as before (7, 7, 8, 7 on a 40-row file); `/api/call` untouched; a deck-sized
  CSV reads in about 0.3 s in the browser instead of 0.75 s. **The one trade:** a month-first CSV whose
  every date has both parts twelve or under and nothing else to show it (never a real deck) is read
  day-first like any sheet with no evidence. **Checked:** importer tests for the four; one table that holds
  all fourteen importers to the rule in three states each; a source scan that fails if any date is read under
  `api/` with no order; the four through the real upload handler; a source pin of the page's CSV read;
  `tools/browser-checks/upload-csv-dates.mjs` on the real reader in a real browser (12 points pass; 6 fail on
  the page as it was); 22 deliberate breaks, each caught; `npm test`: 1,592 pass. **Not done, said plainly:**
  the order is still decided per request (a slice of a big file carries thousands of dates; nothing is
  carried between slices); stored rows keep their dates (RUN-ME-060 for the decks; LAST TRANS DATE not
  repaired); a file re-saved in a spreadsheet program may carry real dates that program already read in its
  own order, which nothing in it can reveal; the four sheets' answers do not say which order they read;
  nothing run on the live book.
- **#716 — the report and the phone list the day's morning deck, as the dashboard card does (handbook
  Part 88).** "Should the report and the phone also leave out customers who fall into default after
  the morning file, so the dashboard card, the report and the phone all count the same people?" —
  "YES" — the owner, 16 Nov, answering the question #715 left open. **Before:** on the card's own test
  data the card read 3 customers / 180,000 owed / 90,000 recovered / 50.0%, and the report 4 /
  279,999 / 90,000 / 32.1%. The one difference was a customer in default on the current file who was
  never on the day's initial file: the card counts initial customers only (Part 74); the report and
  the phone listed the evening's new defaulters with their current arrears as their "initial",
  recovered 0. **Now** (`buildRows_`, only when the day's two decks are a pair, Part 87): that customer
  is not a row of the day, on the report's day, a leader's card, the phone's list and strip, and every
  filed day of the week; the same data reads 3 / 180,000 / 90,000 / 50.0 on both. `enteredAfter`
  (`{customers, arrears}`) on the report's day view and in the phone's `diag` says how many were left
  out (null with no pair); the page does not draw it. **Left alone on purpose:** the Expected
  Defaulters tab (the weekly-cycle book of every defaulter, not a day's list) and the all-time ghost
  (Part 71, ordered 13 Nov), which is still counted in the report's and the phone's figures and is not
  on the card: **the only thing the three still differ on** (section 8). **The consequence to know:** a
  team missing from a day's initial file while its current file carries it reads as "entered default
  after the morning" and leaves a multi-team reader's day (the whole-book rule on the initial side,
  RUN-ME-032); `enteredAfter` shows it, and a one-team reader is not touched. **Cost:** no read; fewer
  rows. **Checked:** the existing tests that relied on the old rule rewritten (one of mine, from #714),
  an evening-only customer added to the four-screens, unpaired, parity and phone tests, five deliberate
  breaks each caught (the customer left on the day, nobody counted as left out, the ghost left out too,
  left out with no pair, the week keeping later entrants); `npm test`: 1,581 pass. Server only: `BUILD`
  and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged. **Not done, said plainly:** ghosts in the figures (section 8); the tab;
  a note on the page; nothing run on the live book, no SQL.
- **#715 — the dashboard card and the Exp.Def Report pinned to one reading; the cost measured; the one
  difference left, named (handbook Part 87 addendum).** After #714 merged, the report's day was laid
  beside the card's tile on the card's own test data: **recovered is the same to the shilling (90,000)**, and
  the percentage is not (50.0% on the card, 32.1% on the report) because one customer who entered default
  after the morning deck is on the current file and not on the day's initial file: the card does not count
  them (Part 74), the report and the phone list them with their current arrears as their initial. Not
  changed: whether "the lists per day come from initial files" extends to the report and the phone was the
  owner's to say (section 8): **he said YES the same evening, #716**. A new test (`test/portal.test.mjs`) pins the card and the report to the same
  customers, arrears, recovery and percentage for a day and its week, in every state a customer can be in;
  three deliberate breaks of #714's rule each turn it red by itself. **Cost of #714, measured at the live
  book's size** (10,372 customers on the morning deck, 4,000 gone from the current one): about 15 ms of CPU
  on the web server for a reading of the whole book, a fortieth of it for one leader; nothing on the
  database. `npm test`: 1,581 pass. Tests and words only: no code of the system changed, `BUILD` and
  `CALL_BUILD` unchanged.
- **#714 — a customer the day's current deck no longer names stays on every list of that day, as
  recovered (handbook Part 87).** "how comes tou said "should disappear." yet we are fighting
  allover to say cleared people shouldnt dissapear for verifications" — the owner, 16 Nov, the
  third time of the same order (13 Nov: "they should stay there as of recovery so as to know
  recovery amount from difference of initial to current per each day by team or staff"; 15 Nov:
  "i expect that customer in initial never to get lost even if cleared its amounts and statuses
  that change"). **What was wrong:** Parts 69 to 82 each closed one door and left the next, so
  one customer — on a day's initial file, absent from its current file — read four ways: the
  dashboard card, recovered in full; the Expected Defaulters tab, in full if never on a current
  file and 0 with the last arrears if on an earlier one; the Exp.Def Report's day, its card and
  the phone, CLEARED with recovered 0 and the old arrears (or not listed at all if never on a
  current file); the report's week, on a board of its own outside every book and total. **The
  belief behind it was wrong** (Part 82 found it on the tab): the rotation does not need a
  current-deck row; the customer's row on the day's own initial deck carries the status, days,
  dates, name and number. **Now one rule, written once in `buildRows_` (`expdf.js`):** given the
  day's own initial rows, a customer on them whom the day's current deck does not name is a row
  built from their MORNING row — CLEARED, nothing owed, the whole of the morning's arrears
  recovered, placed by the rotation off that row, so the leader credited is the leader who held
  them — on the report's day, the leader's card, the phone's list and strip, and every filed day
  of the week (Part 78's board is empty by construction); a ghost for a customer on the morning
  deck is dropped for the morning row (the ghost is an older last-known row); a ghost for a
  customer NOT on it is Part 71 unchanged; on the tab (`expectedDefaulters`) the same ghost is
  dropped so the tab agrees. **Only on a whole pair:** the day's initial and current decks must be
  the same date (a weekday's latest current deck can be last week's, before today's is uploaded),
  or nobody is marked paid on a comparison that was never made (the answers say which it was:
  `paired` on the report's day view and in the phone's `diag`). Only customers whose cycle day it
  is, as ever. **Cost:** no read anywhere — arithmetic over the two decks each screen already
  reads; `test/call.test.mjs` counts the phone's reads with and without such customers (equal)
  and `test/speed.test.mjs` counts the five Exp.Def screens with and without twenty-five (equal);
  the week's initial deck is read for ten columns instead of four, the same rows. **Checked:**
  eight tests (the old Part 78 test rewritten; one rule on four screens; the unpaired deck; team
  scoping; three through the real call dispatch; the speed test), and ten deliberate breaks of
  the rule on the portal side (eight caught, two not changes of behaviour: said in Part 87), three
  on the phone, two on the zero-read promise — all caught; a first set missed the morning row
  being placed by the CLEARED override, so a chronic customer on the morning deck was added.
  `npm test`: 1,580 pass. Server only: `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged. **What the owner will
  see:** the report's recovered totals, a leader's card and the phone's EXP.DEF % go up by exactly
  the customers on the morning list and not on the evening's, toward the dashboard card's figure.
  **The trade it carries:** a team missing from a day's current file reads as recovered in full on
  the report and the phone too, as on the card since #707 (section 8). **Not done, said
  plainly:** a reader whose teams are ALL missing from the day's current file (a small team whose
  last defaulter paid) gets nothing closed — the scope's date is lost when it has no rows
  (section 8); the hours before the day's current file is in are as they were (the tab still lists
  a customer new on today's initial file as CLEARED in that gap: section 8); the month view and
  the old `weekly` baseline are not changed; the page's board for Part 78 (`xd_unassigned`) is
  dormant code, left for the next change to that page; nothing run on the live book, no SQL.
- **#713 — the stored swapped dates put right in place, with no re-upload (handbook Part 86;
  `db/RUN-ME-060-repair-swapped-deck-dates.sql`, pasted in full in the chat, 16 Nov).** "we cant
  re-upload now - i hope the system autoupdates whats right brother" — the owner, 16 Nov, the
  evening #711 and #712 merged. **What it does:** changes DISB DATE, EXPIRE DATE and CHRONIC DATE
  of defaulter deck rows whose stored dates are the day/month swap of what the loan system sent,
  and nothing else, for the days its first two lines name (today, to begin with), at most 20,000
  rows a run; safe to run again, and it says how many rows still wait (`still_to_do_run_again`).
  **How it knows, with nothing guessed:** two witnesses the date bug never touched, both measured
  on the real 16 Nov file (10,372 loans, April 2024 to November 2026): a loan expires 91 days
  after it was disbursed and turns chronic 14 days after it expires (10,372 of 10,372), and DAYS
  ELAPSED is the days from the disbursement date to the deck's day less 2 (10,372 of 10,372; 1 or
  2 across the decks of the owner's loan), which names the real disbursement date to within a few
  days while a swapped one is at least 27 days away. **A row is changed only when its three dates
  as stored do not fit all of that and exactly one reading of them (each date as stored, or with
  its day and month exchanged) fits all of it.** A restructured loan, a row with a date or DAYS
  ELAPSED missing, a row where no reading or two fit: left as it is. **The first form was wrong and
  the test found it:** with the 91-and-14 pattern alone, a loan whose expiry was moved by the days
  a swap moves its disbursement date (30, for 4 March and 3 April) fit it under a swapped date —
  150 rows of the test book changed to something that is not the truth; DAYS ELAPSED, the second
  witness, rejects it. **Checked:** over what the old importer stored for the real file, 8,296
  loans had a wrong date, all 8,296 were put right to what the file says, none changed wrongly,
  and the 2,076 already right were left alone; the kept check `tools/db-checks/deck-date-repair.mjs`
  on a PostgreSQL (3,000 loans, 85,905 deck rows, fifteen days, loans made to fool the rule): 47,120
  repaired, none changed to anything but the truth, no right date made wrong, a second run changed
  0, forty runs of 3,000 ended where one big run did, the file's own undo put every row back (the
  same md5), and with the DAYS ELAPSED lines taken out the same check sees 1,770 rows changed
  wrongly and refuses to pass; `test/deck-date-repair.test.mjs` pins the file's safety lines in
  `npm test`. **Cost:** reads the named days through the `snapshot_date` index and writes only the
  rows it changes (14,935 rows in 1.4 s on a test book of 297,015 deck rows with the live table's
  two trigram indexes); a log table nothing reads (`defaulter_date_repair_log`: the old and new
  dates, and the undo at the foot of the file); a 50-second statement limit and a 3-second lock
  limit, so a run that is cut off changes nothing; nothing on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`; no
  `BUILD` change. **Not done, said plainly:** LAST TRANS DATE was swapped the same way and has no
  pattern to be checked against, so it is NOT repaired here (tonight's Current upload, and every
  file from now on, carries it right and refreshes the Follow-up list); the Expected sheet's dates
  (`repayment_snapshots`, read by no screen) are not touched; **nobody has run it on the live book**
  (a chat cannot): the figures above are the real file's, on a test book. **What the owner has to
  do:** section 8, first bullet.
- **Staff registry, 16 Nov — three people from one WhatsApp paste, all run by the owner and
  resolved (no name is committed, and none is in this file).** (1) A Lake Coast field officer
  whose form said F.O, filed in the registry under a manager's role on Buzwagi A (the 15 Nov
  conflict below): a PLACE wrote the form's role and team, and the person now reads `Field Officer
  - BUZWAGI A`. (2) A manager of Himo (North Coast) who said he cannot see his name: the script
  answered MOVED to `Manager - HIMO` and the team carries its own ZONE, so the registry row is not
  what is wrong; the advance request tab offers a name by the VIEWER's role words and zone, so the
  likelier cause is an access code (the role or teams it carries, so that the desk reads no zone
  for it), and the owner was told where to look. Not known, not changed on a guess. (3) A new
  Northern-zone field officer: the first script found him ALREADY IN THE REGISTRY under a
  placeholder role (the form's "Northern zone" is not a filed zone; NORTH is), so a PLACE with the
  role `Field Officer` and the sender's word NORTH moved him: now Field Officer in NORTH, Active.
  A TIN and an NSSF sent as "on process" were left out of the scripts, as always.
- **`CHAT.md` named staff, and no longer does (16 Nov).** The repository is public and section 2
  says no staff name is committed, yet the entries of 13 to 15 Nov named officers and carried a
  staff ID. They are rewritten without a name (the same facts); the names remain in git history,
  which a change to this file cannot rewrite. **Older files from before the order still hold
  names and were not touched:** `api/_lib/annual-holders-data.js` (first names of the managers a
  team had, for the annual deck) and the fixtures of `test/importers.test.mjs` (names and account
  numbers that may be real; nobody has checked). Whether they stay, and whether the history is
  rewritten, is the owner's (section 8). Also corrected in the 15 Nov entry below: a PLACE writes
  the role and team its row carries and fills only the EMPTY details; it is a NEW that finds the
  person and changes nothing.
- **#712 — the Expected and Follow-up uploads read the file's own date order too (handbook Part
  85).** "so fix date readings okay? i even seen the error on default lists at disb date
  columns" — the owner, 16 Nov, the hour #711 was merged. **The page was not the fault:** a list's
  Disb date column prints what is stored (`String(v).slice(0, 10)`), so the error on the lists was
  the stored date that #711 stopped writing. Two lists show a Disb date: Expected Defaulters (from
  the defaulters decks) and the **Follow-up list** (`followup_status`), which every **Current**
  deck refreshes (`writeFollowupFromDeck`; an Initial file never touches it) **and** the
  "Defaulters Followup" upload writes. That importer, like the Expected sheet's (`importExpected`,
  into `repayment_snapshots`; no screen reads those columns yet), read its dates with no order
  (found by sweeping every `dateOrNull(` that has no second argument). A Followup file uploaded
  after a corrected Current deck would have put the swapped Disb date and Last trans back. Now each
  sheet's own date columns are pooled and read by the same rule (`expectedDateOrder`,
  `followupDateOrder`, `inferDayFirst`); the upload's answer says `dateOrder`, and a sentence only
  when a sheet gave no clue. **#711's note "nothing reads what they store" was wrong for the
  Follow-up list**; it is corrected in Part 84 and below. **Cost:** CPU only, no read or write
  added: a 40-row Expected deck is 21 round trips and a Followup file 8, **the same on the code as
  merged and on this change**; `/api/call` untouched; `BUILD` and `CALL_BUILD` unchanged.
  **Checked:** seven importer tests and four through the real upload handler, four deliberate
  breaks each caught; `npm test` 1,568 pass. **Not done, said plainly:** stored rows keep their
  swapped dates (the Follow-up list is put right for a customer by the next Current deck they are
  on, today's once it is uploaded again; past days by their own files); **an exact repair of the
  stored rows was not built here: it is #713, above**; the complaints and restructures
  sheets (the company's own forms, not the loan system's export) read their dates with no order
  and nobody has reported them; `migrate/` reads month-first by construction and is not wrong.
- **#711 — the defaulters upload read the loan system's month-first dates day-first (handbook
  Part 84).** "Was 2209966593 on initial file of today 16th nov 2026 & expected defaulters? is it
  still in the expected defaulter reports for the recovered amounts?" — the owner, 16 Nov, who
  ran a read-only query and then sent the file itself. **It was on the file, and it is a Monday
  customer** (disbursed `9/7/2026`, month-first, so Monday 7 September: Monday and Thursday);
  **the book held 9 July** (Thursday and Sunday), so it was never on a Monday list. The loan
  system writes every date M/D/YYYY as text; `importDefaulters` read `dateOrNull(value)` with no
  order, so every date with both numbers 12 or under was stored with day and month swapped. **The
  scale, on the real 10,372-loan file:** 37% of every date column; 3,683 loans on the wrong cycle
  days; Monday's list missing 1,204 real Monday customers and carrying 898 that are not;
  **3,461 of 9,234 expired or chronic customers handed to the wrong recycling leader or week**
  (their owner is read off weeks since the expiry or chronic date). This is a large part of why
  leaders' lists never matched what their GMO or OPM expected. **The rule already existed**
  ("Day/month order is read off the file, not assumed": `inferDayFirst`, used by the comment,
  payment, notice, application and loan importers) and the defaulters importer, the most
  important one, never got it. Now `defaultersDateOrder` pools the evidence of the deck's five
  date columns and every date is read that way; only a deck that PROVES month-first reads
  differently (a deck with no clue, or one that proves day-first, is read as before); the upload
  answers `defaulterDateOrder`, and says so in its message only when it had to guess. **Cost:**
  CPU only, about 16 ms on the real deck; no read, no write, same 20 trips on the test
  database; `/api/call` untouched; `BUILD`/`CALL_BUILD` unchanged. **Checked:** the real file
  through the real importer before and after (3,883 swapped dates → 0; wrong cycle days 3,683 →
  0; rotation changes 3,461 → 0; Monday 3,842 against 4,148 → 4,148 against 4,148); six
  importer tests and three through the real upload handler, three of them failing with the
  reading taken out; `npm test` 1,557 pass. **What the owner has to do:** rows already in the
  book keep their swapped dates (only the file knows which), so **upload today's Initial AND
  Current files again after the deploy** (a newer upload replaces the older whole), and earlier
  days only if the week's figures must be exact (**since #713 the stored dates are put right in
  place and no upload is needed**); when they are put right about a third of the
  customers move to their real days and the leaders' lists change. **Not done, said plainly:**
  (1) after the re-read, the loan of the question is a Monday customer absent from the current
  file, and **the screens still disagree about such a customer** — the dashboard card counts it
  recovered in full; the Expected Defaulters tab does when it never was on a current file; the
  Exp.Def Report and the phone list it CLEARED with recovered 0 (and its last arrears) when it
  was on an earlier current file, and not at all when it never was (the week keeps it on a
  separate board outside the totals): found while answering, a design change that moves the
  report's recovered totals and the phone's EXP.DEF %, so the owner's to say (section 8); (2)
  `importExpected` and `importFollowup` read dates the old way (fixed in #712; nothing read what the
  Expected sheet stores, but the Follow-up list does show what the Followup sheet stores);
  (3) the order is read per request, so a last slice of a few loans may carry no evidence and
  reads day-first (and says so). **A reusable read-only probe is kept:**
  `db/TEMPLATE-where-is-this-customer-read-only.sql` (a loan number and a day: on which files,
  on whose list, and every disbursement date it has carried). A first version of it flagged the
  rows of the upload that counts as "superseded", because it compared single rows and an upload
  is written in chunks with different timestamps; the owner ran it on the real book and the
  answer was wrong in that column (and only that one). It decides the winner as the portal does,
  by the newest row's batch, and was re-tested on an upload written in two chunks.
- **#710 — a leader's own list, and the teams their own code cannot see (handbook Part 83).**
  "gmo or opm is seeing expected defaulters of gmo or manager where that manager in their code
  ain't seeing them, they getting just a little like two yet a Manager's gmo sees the manager
  has many ... so make sure the distribution is okay" — the owner, 16 Nov. **The distribution is
  one rule and is not what differs:** a new test reads every leader of every role off the GMO's
  report and then the same person's own list with a code that holds their teams — the same
  customers, arrears and recovery to the shilling. **What differs is who is reading.** The
  GMO's code holds the zone; the manager's holds what the owner typed on it, and the owner's rule
  ("the teams i set in access codes are the ones correct") is that the code decides what is seen
  and the sheet never widens it. When the sheet names a manager on six teams and her code holds
  two, the report credits her six teams and her screen shows two — reproduced as 12 on the
  report, 4 on her phone, 16 of 48 on the portal — and nothing said why. **That rule is not
  changed; the silence is.** The portal's Expected Defaulters and the phone's Exp.Def strip name
  the teams the sheet gives the person that their code does not hold (`outside`, from teams
  already read); the Exp.Def Report has an **Off code** column and a note on each board, said
  only where exactly one access code carries the leader's name; one person is one row
  (`leaderKey` in `api/_lib/leader-scope.js`: runs of blanks as one — `MARY  M` and `MARY M`
  were two rows of the report and the manager's own list found only one of them; a name that
  differs in a WORD is not merged, a person settles that). **`db/RUN-ME-059-leaders-codes-vs-
  sheet-read-only.sql`** (pasted in full in the chat, 16 Nov) lists every such person off the
  live book: codes that lack teams the sheet names them on, codes with extra teams, sheet names
  no code carries (with the closest codes), two codes of one name; no access code is printed.
  **Cost:** no read added; `/api/upload` untouched; on `/api/call` arithmetic over teams already
  held; the report reads the codes `weekWork` already read, one column wider; `test/
  speed.test.mjs` unchanged. **Checked:** 9 new tests, 7 deliberate breaks each caught; two
  kept browser checks (`portal-expdef-offcode.mjs`, 30 points; `call-expdef-offcode.mjs`, 10);
  the query on a PostgreSQL 16 copy of the table shapes, 3 ms. `npm test`: 1,548 pass. `BUILD
  2026-11-16-c`, `CALL_BUILD 2026-11-16-a`. **Not done:** nothing widens a leader's list past
  their code (offered, section 8); the bike share of a team that names no bike officer is
  nobody's by the existing rotation (the report already says so); the phone's list is today's
  cycle and the report's week/month are the whole book, by design; whether this is what the
  live book has could not be seen from here.
- **#709 — Expected Defaulters: a cleared customer keeps their owner role, recycling leader and
  cycle (handbook Part 82).** "Don't clean owner role, recycling leader & cycle for 'cleared'
  customers at expected defaulters because the disb date still gives us where to place them
  for the 3 columns - we need those 3 filled for followup purposes" — the owner, 16 Nov. The
  rows that came empty were the ones added for a customer on this morning's initial file who is
  on no current row (Part 75): role, phase and cycle empty, leader "(unassigned)", and no name
  or number either, on the belief that the rotation needs fields only a current row has. It
  does not: the initial file is imported by the same importer as the current one, so the
  morning row carries the status, the days elapsed and the dates `assignFor` reads. Every row
  of the list is now built by one function (`rowOf`); the three columns are where the rotation
  places the customer today, the name, number, D.S and D.C are the morning row's, and a leader
  finds the customer under "My list". CLEARED, nothing owed and the whole morning's arrears
  recovered stand as before. **Cost:** nine more columns of rows already read; no read added;
  `test/speed.test.mjs` unchanged. **Checked:** the row laid beside the same customer on the
  current file reads the same role, cycle, phase and leader; both tests fail with the columns
  emptied again. `npm test`: 1,539 pass. Server only; `BUILD` unchanged. **Not done:** the
  Exp.Def Report week view's separate board "Cleared, no leader on record for the day" (the
  same reasoning could credit those customers to a leader inside the report: it changes the
  report's totals and was not asked).
- **#708 — the advance desk, and the leave desk, save a big tick in a few trips (handbook
  Part 81).** "someone is saying ticking multiple advance requests to approve at once fails
  unless one by one" — the owner, 16 Nov. **The page was not the fault:** a new kept check
  (`tools/browser-checks/portal-advance-bulk.mjs`) ticks three, then three hundred, in a real
  browser and watches the one call go out with every id; the Android app shows the confirm
  question too. **The server could not carry a big tick:** `advDecide` read the ticked rows
  with every id in one address (a uuid is thirty-seven bytes of URL; three hundred are eleven
  thousand, and gateways commonly refuse about eight) and then saved each request with its own
  trip, one after another, inside the page's forty-five seconds; when that failed, part was
  already saved and the list on screen did not show it. Now the rows are read sixty ids at a
  time and saved one statement a chunk for each answer and comment, `where id in (chunk) and
  approval in (as it was read)`: three hundred ticks are ten trips where they were three
  hundred and one, a request somebody else decided meanwhile is left as they decided it, and a
  chunk refused after others were saved answers "N NOT SAVED, tick them again" instead of
  throwing the saved ones away. `leaveDecide` is the same code and got the same change. On the
  page: the buttons are off and the screen says "Saving N…" while it runs; the list is read
  again after any answer; a tick of a request no longer pending is dropped. **Not known:** the
  person's screen and the words of the red message were not seen, so which limit they met, and
  with how many ticks, is not known; three ticks worked before and work now; the address limit
  is from the code and common gateways, not tried on the live one. **Cost:** a bulk decision
  was one read and N writes, now one of each for every sixty requests; nothing on
  `/api/upload` or `/api/call`. **Checked:** tests (150 ticks are three statements; the race;
  two answers; a refused chunk), each failing with the chunking out; the browser check's 36
  points with 8 and with 300 requests. `BUILD 2026-11-16-b`. **Not done:** the phone
  register's bulk actions (a list of IMEIs in one address) and the imprest approvals were not
  looked at.

  crashes node's own test runner and cascades to fail every later test in the file; the fix
  mirrors an existing, already-safe pattern in this same test file (a mock that answers late
  instead of never). No new read, no change to round-trip counts either way (`test/speed.test.mjs`

  guarantors given (a mother, a sister) fit the registry's two kin slots and need no choice
  between them. Account, TIN and NSSF are all "on process" and so left out, as always. Nothing
  is filed until the role and team come back. The name is in no file of the repository.
- **The Loan pipeline widget leaves; Expected Defaulters recovery, Mon–Sun, arrives, 14 Nov**
  — "Remove the Loan pipeline widget at dashboard replace with expected defaulters recovery
  cards Monday to Sunday with rising and falling arrows as usual"; asked whether that meant
  the per-officer figure the Exp.Def Report shows or a team-wide tile, the owner answered "Am
  talking of the days performance Monday to Sunday like the cards showing summary of sales
  and collection per day" — team-wide, confirmed. A new database function
  (`db/RUN-ME-056`, `expdf_recovery_totals`) answers, per team per day, who counts as an
  Expected Defaulter (`buildRows_`'s own membership test, ported, never the rotation that
  decides WHO owns a customer — re-deriving that in SQL would be a second, driftable copy of
  a rule CLAUDE.md section 2 already names two blackouts for) and what they recovered — a sum
  of per-customer, never-negative differences, which is why this card can read a different
  number from "Weekly recovery trend" beside it on the same day, on purpose, proved directly
  in a new test. Measured on a 2.27-million-row fixture before it shipped (42 teams, 150 days,
  matching RUN-ME-054/055's own scale): ~210ms worst case, ~43ms the common one-team case, one
  round trip either way — a first fixture with team-colliding customer references understated
  this badly (collapsed 42 teams into 1) until it was rebuilt with globally-unique ones, the
  realistic shape this system's own `ref` column always has. Costs **net +1 round trip**
  (+2 new reads, -1 removed: the pipeline funnel's own `stageCounts` call leaves with the
  widget it existed for), 0 new rows, on the migrated-world dashboard budget
  (`test/speed.test.mjs`, reason beside the changed rows); no read on `/api/upload` or
  `/api/call`. `npm test`: 1,530 pass (one new test, mirroring the SQL clause for clause in a
  new `EXPDF_RECOVERY_RPC` mock, the same idiom the ghost-rows mock already uses). `BUILD
  2026-11-14-b`; `CALL_BUILD` untouched. Handbook Part 73.
- **Early Collection joins the week, 14 Nov** — the strip tile is week-over-week with the same
  rise/fall arrow every other tile carries, reversing #691's own month-to-date choice in the
  owner's own words ("Early Collection % · MTD should be of week and showing rise or fall as the
  other widgets"); the standalone card is now a five-day Monday–Friday grid, built to the exact
  pattern "loan apps, sales, col and rec" already share, each day its own arrow against the same
  weekday a week earlier (#690's own rule). Built by reading one more pair of weeks through
  `expectedTotalsInRange`, the same function `expWeek`/`expPrev`/`colTrend` already call — this
  week unbounded, last week budgeted under `dashPrevBudget_`, the identical asymmetry those two
  already carry and for the same reason written beside them. The new figures are no longer read
  off the month ledger, so they are no more migration-sensitive than the three strip deltas they
  now sit beside — `test/snapshot-totals.test.mjs`'s own parity check verifies them for free, and
  the list that used to carve them out of it is removed outright. **Costs +2 round trips, 0 new
  rows**, on the migrated-world dashboard budget only (`test/speed.test.mjs`, reason beside each
  changed row); no read on `/api/upload` or `/api/call`. Checked: `test/portal.test.mjs`'s
  per-day-arrow test and the strip's own team-scoping test, both extended/rewritten rather than
  added, so `npm test` holds at 1,529. No real-browser check — no database to sign into from
  here; `app.html`'s inline script is still parsed by `test/pages.test.mjs`, part of `npm test`.
  `BUILD 2026-11-14-a`; `CALL_BUILD` untouched. Handbook Part 72.
- **Staff registry, 13 Nov (sixth)** — three officers of Team Buzwagi A and B the P.M.O
  Supervisor flagged as not seeing themselves on their own team's list ("SIWAONI") -- the same
  flag, and the same PLACE handling, as the four Chamwino/Moro Center/Kihonda officers of 12
  Nov. Zone Lake Coast was given directly for the whole message; neither team is on Teams &
  Staff yet, so the sender's word is what settled it, the same as both South West batches
  needed the same day (below). No phone, account, NIDA or NSSF was sent for any of the three,
  and none is invented in their place. Tested against a synthetic registry with one of the
  three already filed elsewhere with no team at all, confirmed moved correctly with the sparse
  data given, and confirmed idempotent.
- **Staff registry, 13 Nov (fifth)** — the PLACE follow-up to the South West batch below: of
  the nineteen, the first NEW run found ten already in the registry rather than new -- the
  house rule for exactly this ("A NEW that comes back ALREADY IN THE REGISTRY is asked again as
  PLACE") was followed at once, without waiting to be asked. Eight were filed with the team
  written straight into the role, without "Team" in front of it ("Manager - LINDI") or, for two
  of them, with no team at all ("MANAGER"); all eight move onto "Team Lindi" / "Team Ruangwa" /
  "Team Newala" / "Team Nachingwea," the way every other team in the registry is named, zone

  account number -- the registry already holds him under a spelling two letters
  off his own name in each of two words; PLACE does not touch that spelling, the same as the
  first run did not, and nothing here decides for the owner whether the two spellings are one
  person -- they plainly are, by the account number alone, but a script still never corrects a
  filed name on its own say. Tested against a synthetic registry built to match exactly what the
  real run reported (the same role strings, the same account-only match), confirmed idempotent.
- **Staff registry, 13 Nov (fourth)** — nineteen new staff across five new teams (Lindi,
  Ruangwa, Tandahimba, Newala, Nachingwea), sent to the group as "NAOMBA U ADD HAWA STUFF NA

  since South West is already a filed zone elsewhere. One account is at NMB (the Tandahimba
  manager); every other account given is CRDB, including one written with two letters in it,
  kept exactly as given. Thirteen NIDA or NSSF numbers were sent as "PROCESSING"/"ON PROCESS"
  (a number is coming) and three NSSF numbers as "NILL" (not the same thing -- nothing is
  coming); all left blank in the script rather than written as that literal text. None of the
  nineteen gave a TIN at all. Every field was checked against the original message by an
  independent pass before the script was built, then the whole batch was tested twice against
  a synthetic registry sharing the real schema (added once; a second run changes nothing and

- **Staff registry, 13 Nov (third)** — a new-hire form for a Kigoma team, forwarded by its
  manager more than two months after it was dated (prepared 2 Sept, sent 13 Nov). Filed as NEW.
  The form's own "Zone" line named a team, not a zone, as every earlier batch has had
  (docs/HANDOFF.md Parts 50, 52) -- but this time the team itself was not on Teams & Staff at

  was sent, so the birth date is the form's own, with nothing to check it against. TIN is blank
  and NSSF is "on process," both left out of the script rather than written as that literal
  text. Three people were named for the registry's two kin slots (a mother, a sister, and an
  aunt given only as "Mama Mdogo," with no name of her own); the two with a name were filed, in
  the order the form gave them -- the aunt's own name and number are not in the registry, kept
  for the owner's record rather than dropped. Tested against a synthetic registry sharing the

  (added once; a second run changes nothing and says so) after it. The name is in no file of
  the repository.
- **#697** — the all-time ghost: a customer missing from every deck still shows, status CLEARED
  (handbook Part 71). "Checked loan system -- customer not there on thurrsday deck 12th nov
  2026" ... "should reflect to alltime data past present and future" ... "so status column of
  such customers automatic becomes cleared if no longer in default list" -- the owner, on ref
  4231948032, the real case #693/#696 could not reach: gone from BOTH the initial AND current
  deck entirely, with nothing in today's own data pointing at them at all. `db/RUN-ME-055` adds
  one function (`defaulter_ghost_rows`) answering "who has this team ever had as a defaulter,
  whose newest row is not today's" -- a loose-index-scan recursive CTE PostgREST's own query
  builder cannot express cheaply (1.6ms vs 67-134ms for a plain SELECT DISTINCT on a
  2.27-million-row fixture), called via `db.rpc(...)`, asked rarely and cached the same shape
  `adjustments.js` already uses for the Iliyonasia register on this same call path
  (`GHOST_TTL_MS` 90s, `GHOST_MISSING_TTL_MS` 5 min, `GHOST_BUDGET_MS` 800ms honoured only by the
  sacred path). Status CLEARED is unconditional for a ghost row -- the absence IS the signal --
  but the numbers stay honest: their own last-known arrears, not a fabricated zero, recovered
  reads 0 since nothing happened today. **Three real mistakes caught building this, before any
  of it shipped:** (1) a first draft called the new function once PER TEAM, which
  `test/speed.test.mjs` caught at 47 round trips against a 7-trip budget the moment an
  unrestricted admin's full team list was looped -- fixed to one round trip per request,
  however many teams; (2) the sort: a ghost's own last-known arrears can be large (15,500 for
  this ref), so the EXISTING "0 sorts last" rule (#696) would have promoted a long-untracked
  customer above one genuinely owing money today -- fixed to CLEARED-last, arrears-descending
  within each group; (3) the feature first refused to run at all for an unrestricted admin
  (`user.teams` null), which would have hidden it from the owner's own likeliest sign-in --
  fixed to resolve the full team list already fetched for other reasons, with the per-request
  round trip and the budget (not a team-count limit) carrying the real safety. Checked through
  the real call dispatch and through `expectedDefaulters`, and separately confirmed absent (no
  failed request) on a database without the migration run. `test/speed.test.mjs`:
  `expectedDefaulters` moves 7 -> 8 trips, the one genuinely necessary new round trip, reason in
  the same diff; every `expdfReport`/`expdfLeader`/`expdfMine` variant had headroom already and
  needed no change. No front-end file touched; `BUILD`/`CALL_BUILD` untouched. **Not done, at
  that point:** `test/speed.test.mjs` had no dedicated budget for `api_callList(...,
  'expdf')` itself (only `'today'`/`'defaulters'`) -- a pre-existing gap this change reaches but
  does not close, flagged rather than expanding scope.
  **Reviewed by an adversarial pipeline before this merged** -- eleven candidate findings,
  each independently re-verified against the real files and a real local Postgres; eight real,
  three refuted. Fixed: `p_known_refs` was being sent as `[]` always (the SQL function's own
  exclusion never ran, so every cache refresh paid its documented worst case -- a disk spill,
  ~7x slower -- not only the rare case it names); `balance` was missing from the function's own
  column list entirely, so a ghost's balance silently read as a fabricated 0 in production, a
  documented field (`docs/REPORT-PAYLOADS.md`) nobody had tested because the test suite's own
  mock was looser than real Postgres and spread the whole fixture row instead of projecting to
  it; the Exp.Def week's own per-day loop never called the ghost lookup at all, so the feature
  vanished with no note switching from day/month to week -- fixed to reach the week only through
  its own live-today iteration, never a closed week (today's gap is not a fact about a week that
  already ended); `ghostTeams` was never deduplicated (a hand-typed access-code team list
  repeating one team double-counted that team's ghosts); and nothing exercised the budget race's
  own timeout-abandonment, the one property the sacred path's safety depends on (confirmed: a
  regression that drops it passes every other test). All fixed, each with a new or corrected
  test that fails on the regression. Refuted: an exact-team-match concern (the function never
  sees a raw, user-typed team name), a cache-key separator concern (not practically reachable),
  and a second team-case-normalization concern at the same line as the dedup fix (a
  normalization layer upstream the finding had not traced back to). Full writeup, including the
  one true nit taken alongside (STABLE instead of VOLATILE, zero behaviour change), is
  handbook Part 71. `npm test`: 1,529 pass (two new tests). `BUILD`/`CALL_BUILD` still untouched.
- **#696** — the worklists keep a cleared customer too, sunk to the bottom, not off the list
  (handbook Part 70). "So even in call app if they clear they go down with status cleared..
  same for the rest defaulters lists if possible" -- the owner, right after confirming the
  TABORA TOWN case (below) was the loan system's own gap, not this one's. #693's own worklist
  exclusion ("nobody should be shown a call to make on an account that is already settled") is
  reversed: `expdfMine` no longer excludes a cleared row, which now sinks to the bottom of the
  phone's own list and the portal's on the arrears-descending sort it already had (0 sorts
  last) -- no new sort, no new field. The status label is renamed `RECOVERED` -> `CLEARED`
  everywhere (`buildRows_`), matching the owner's own word -- and, checked rather than assumed,
  both `call.html`'s and `app.html`'s own shared status-chip classifiers already render
  `CLEARED` green, and the phone's own Exp.Def list filters by role or day, never by status, so
  the call app needed NO change at all; `CALL_BUILD` is untouched. `expectedDefaulters`
  (portal-core.js, the "Expected Defaulters" tab -- the other worklist #693 named and
  deliberately left alone) gets the identical relaxation `buildRows_` already has, word for
  word the same condition -- one rule, read twice, which is not the same promise as "the two
  can never disagree": `expectedDefaulters` resolves "today" through the latest deck of ANY
  weekday, `buildRows_` through `expdfCore`'s own weekday-PINNED resolution (falls back to last
  week's same weekday when today's has not landed), a deliberate pre-existing split this change
  does not close -- on a morning the day's deck is late, the same customer can read CLEARED on
  one tab and be entirely absent from the other three. The one front-end touch is `app.html`'s
  own `defexp` screen: its screen-local status chip (CHRONIC/EXPIRED only, everything else flat
  grey) gained a CLEARED branch, its status filter gained the option, and its "In the weekly
  cycle" KPI's own words were corrected, since it now counts anyone cleared too.
  `BUILD 2026-11-13-f`.
  **Reviewed by two independent readers before this merged**, given it reverses a decision on
  the sacred call path: one real bug. CLEARED was decided by status-TEXT elimination alone, no
  check of `arrears` -- a blank or unrecognised status (the suite's own long-standing `'555'`
  fixture: no status, partially paid, 350 still owed) came back CLEARED, rendered green, and
  SORTED FIRST on an officer's list (plain arrears-descending sort; a mislabelled CLEARED row
  with real arrears is promoted, not sunk) -- while genuinely owing money. This codebase already
  has the one rule for "is this actually paid" (`api/_lib/settled.js`'s `isSettled`, written
  after a real customer sat on a phone as a defaulter owing one shilling while the expected book
  had her overpaid); this change had reinvented a wrong one instead of reading the right one.
  Fixed: `isSettled(arrears)` gates CLEARED in both functions; anything else keeps its own
  status text, or DEFAULTER with none, the same fallback `statusWithPaid` already uses. Two new
  regression tests reproduce the review's own scenario directly. Checked with four tests in all
  (two rewritten for #696's own claim flipping from exclusion to inclusion, two new for the
  review's finding) across `test/portal.test.mjs` and `test/call.test.mjs` -- the latter also
  proving SORT ORDER, a cleared customer sinking below a real defaulter sharing their bucket,
  through the real call dispatch. One real fixture bug caught before any of them passed: two
  customers of the same team/day given different `upload_batch` ids made one lose to the other
  under `pickLatestBatch`'s own per-team rule (a real upload never does this); fixed by sharing
  one batch id, the way an upload actually arrives. No new read anywhere. `npm test`: 1,525
  pass. **Not done:** the weekday-pinned-vs-any-weekday split above, a separate and larger
  question than this one status label; and the harder case #693 named, still not reached -- a
  customer missing from a day's current deck entirely, which the TABORA TOWN case (below) turned
  out to be the loan system's own gap, not a reason to build a backward-looking diff on a guess
  about what it would cost the sacred call path.
- **TABORA TOWN check, 13 Nov — closed, not this codebase.** Ref 4231948032 (Chronic, 15,500
  arrears, days_elapsed 149) sat on no Exp.Def list anywhere -- portal or call app -- for
  Thursday 12 Nov: the real-world instance of the one gap #693 named and left open, a customer
  absent from the day's own current deck entirely rather than present with a changed status.
  The owner checked the loan system itself, not only this database, and confirmed: the customer
  was already missing from the loan system's own 12 Nov deck. Nothing was lost between the loan
  system and this one -- the upload wrote down exactly what it was sent, and `buildRows_` /
  `expdfMine` / `expectedDefaulters` had nothing to find because there was nothing to find.
  Whatever took the customer off that one day's export happened upstream, in the loan system,
  for a reason outside what a chat can see or guess at from here. Not investigated further.

  after) and he was already on Team Chamwino — what actually changed is the ROLE WORD,
  `Bike Recovery - Team Chamwino` to `Field Officer - CHAMWINO`, confirmed against the code

  'RECOVERY' alias to nothing, so a Field-Officer-signed-in code could never see a staff filed

  is left out of the script as always — one more number waiting on section 8, beside two
